## QBO Item List Read — the first read of a customer's chart of items — added 2026-08-29

**What:** `GET /api/qbo/items` — **one read-only `select * from Item`** against the connected
QuickBooks company, returned to the operator's screen. **Nothing is written to Intuit and nothing is
stored on our side.**

---

### THE QUICKBOOKS INVOICE PUSH — THE ItemRef IS DISARMED (cap 4.1 · ledger #237, 2026-08-30)

**Status:** 🟡 BUILDER-COMPLETE — owner-proof owed (CARDS 4 · 15–18) | **Vertical:** cultivar (shared surface) | **Type:** integration / write
**Location:** `packages/shared/src/quickbooks/invoiceLineShapes.ts` (**NEW** — the construct vocabulary +
the ItemRef resolution) + `invoiceLineShapes.test.ts` (**50 assertions**) ·
`packages/cultivar-os/api/qbo/invoice/cultivar.ts` (`buildQboInvoiceLines` + `pushQboInvoice`) ·
`qboInvoiceLines.test.ts` (**61 assertions**) · `invoiceList.ts` repointed at the shared constant.
**ZERO new Vercel functions (12/12) · ZERO schema · ZERO migration.**

🔴 **ONE RULE REPLACES TWELVE HARDCODED LITERALS:** *a $0 line is a **`DescriptionOnly` NOTE** carrying
no ItemRef; a line carrying money is **REVENUE** and must resolve an **Intuit `Item.Id`** off its
backing row, or **the push REFUSES** (422 `QBO_ITEM_UNMAPPED`).* There is **no fallback branch** — a
default is how every tree came to book against a generic income item. The refusal names **every**
unmapped line at once with the money at stake, and **a partially-mapped order refuses the WHOLE
push**: an invoice three-quarters right is silently wrong on the fourth line.

**The twelve were never twelve item-id problems** — five were **$0 documentation lines** booking as $0
SALES of things the customer had DECLINED, one was the discount, one was sales tax.
- ✅ **`DescriptionOnly` and native `DiscountLineDetail` MATCH LAWNS's OWN PRACTICE** — their history
  carries **194** description-only lines and **66** native discount lines worth **$31,985**, three
  times more than their discount ITEMS (21).
- 🔴 **SALES TAX LEFT THE LINE LIST FOR `TxnTaxDetail`.** As a revenue line it **inflated the
  business's income by the tax amount** — money held for the state, recorded as theirs.
- **`PercentBased: false`** is load-bearing: the percent form would discount the SERVICE lines, which
  D-39 forbids. **No `DiscountAccountRef` and no `TaxRateRef` are fabricated** — we hold neither id.
- 🔴 **A SURCHARGE IS NOT A DISCOUNT.** An owner override ABOVE baseline used to flip a sign on a
  "discount"; QuickBooks has no negative discount, so an upcharge now takes the **revenue** path and
  refuses without a mapped id like any other sale.

⚠️ **EVERY REVENUE LINE REFUSES TODAY, AND THAT IS THE HONEST STATE.** No table carries `qbo_item_id`
— that is pass ②, specced at `docs/decisions/2026-08-30-qbo-item-mapping-spec.md` (**three tables, not
one**; it stores the **Id**, never the Sku). The consumer is proven correct *before* the producer
exists: the test's `mapped` fixture is invoice 436 with ids on its rows and produces exactly the
payload ② will make live.

⚠️ **THE HOLD STAYS ON.** Its original cause is gone; it stays because three constructs here have
never been seen by Intuit and a malformed line 400s the WHOLE invoice.

---

### THE QUICKBOOKS ITEM LIST — READ-ONLY (cap 4.1 · ledger #229)
**Status:** 🟡 BUILDER-COMPLETE — owner-proof owed (ledger #229) | **Vertical:** cultivar (shared surface) | **Type:** integration / read
**Location:** `packages/cultivar-os/api/qbo/router.ts` → **`handleItems`** (`_route=items`) ·
`packages/shared/src/quickbooks/itemList.ts` (the pure half) + `itemList.test.ts` (**40 probes**) ·
`packages/shared/src/components/QboItemListReader.tsx` (mounted in the shared Accounting card) ·
`vercel.json` rewrite. **No new Vercel function — it rides the existing QBO router** (§6 r11).

**🔴 WHY IT WAS URGENT.** The invoice push carries **TWELVE hardcoded
`ItemRef: { value: '1', name: 'Services' }` literals**. Nothing had pushed to the live LAWNS company
yet — their books were clean only because Lauren's write permissions blocked it — so **the next
completed checkout is the first real push**, and it would book every line, the trees included, as
generic "Services": 100% service revenue, zero product sales, no COGS against inventory. This read
is what makes the real item ids knowable. **It changes none of the twelve.** ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-30 (#237) — kept because it was true when written: the twelve are now GONE, and what the read settled is that the fix could not have been "a better id". Item `1` EXISTS, is named "Sales" not "Services", and books to their generic income account — so the push would have SUCCEEDED and silently misfiled, not failed. See the ItemRef-disarm entry below.**

**What it returns per item:** `Id · Name · Type · IncomeAccountRef.name · Active`. The **income
account** is the field that actually decides the Sales-of-Nursery-Stock vs Services split — more
than the manual browser-URL method gives.

**🔴 THE RAW BODY IS SAVED TO A FILE, OUTSIDE THE REPO, BEFORE ANYTHING RENDERS.** Every response —
success or failure — is written to the operator's browser download folder as
`qbo-items-<realm>-<timestamp>.json`. Two reasons: **(1)** re-reading a customer's books must never
require re-querying a customer's books; **(2)** the download folder is outside version control, so a
copy of live accounting data cannot be swept into a commit — the same class of hazard as the
`service_role` JWT that sat in a settings file. A parsed table is shown **on top of** the file, never
instead of it.

**Honesty properties, each with a probe behind it:**
- **An empty item list and an unreadable response are DIFFERENT surfaces.** A company genuinely can
  have no items (`QueryResponse` with no `Item` key) — that is a true answer. A body we could not
  parse says so. Rendering the second as the first is this platform's oldest defect and §A of the
  test exists for it alone.
- **A missing field is `null`, rendered "Not set"** — never a plausible guess. Defaulting an absent
  `IncomeAccountRef` would fabricate the exact fact the read exists to establish.
- **401 and 403 name different next steps** (`classifyFailure`): a 401 is the token-refresh path, a
  403 is the granted scope. A generic failure message would send someone hunting the wrong one.
- **The body is never logged.** `[TRACE:QBO]` carries status, realm and a COUNT — never a name, never
  a body, never the token.

**Authority:** `callerCan(auth, business_id, 'settings:read')` — the same string `/api/qbo/status`
takes. 🔴 **The handler reads under the SERVICE KEY, which bypasses RLS entirely**, so
`bas_owner_all` never runs on this path and this gate is the only thing behind it. It resolves the
caller from the **Bearer token, never the body**. Not owner-only: David passes as `owner_id`, and any
member holding `settings:read` passes too.

**⚠️ DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT, so absence is not read as coverage:**
- ~~**Pagination**~~ ✅ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-29 (#230) — AND THE FLAG WAS RIGHT.** The one page came
  back `maxResults: 100, startPosition: 1` with ids past 1127: truncated, silent, caught only
  because David read the ids. Both reads now count first and refuse a shortfall — see the #230
  entry below.
- **Storage** — persisting a customer's chart of items is its own ruling and has not been made.
- **The mapping** — the twelve literals are untouched; that is the next pass.

**Owner test:** `docs/owner-tests/quickbooks-invoice-full-surface-test.md` **CARDS 5–8** (added to the
existing board, not a second document — STD-011). **Nothing marked covered.**
**Story:** `user_stories.md` — *QuickBooks read-back + customer de-dup* (**its premise was corrected
in this same commit**: it claimed the integration was create-only, and D-47 read-back had shipped
2026-07-16).

## Checkout customer email persistence — the shared upsert's UPDATE payload (#217) — added 2026-08-25

**PURPOSE** — an email typed at the register is the address the invoice is SENT to, so the business must
afterwards HOLD it. Board 3.7. Story: `user_stories.md` → *The email the register types is the email the
business holds* (`STATUS: gap`, as-built).

🔴 **THE DEFECT, AND THE MECHANISM WAS AN OMISSION — NOT A FILTER, NOT AN OVERWRITE.**
`packages/shared/src/business-logic/customerUpsert.ts` builds its UPDATE payload from a list of `offer()`
calls (`:136-144`). **Eight fields were offered — `first_name` · `last_name` · `phone` · `address_line1` ·
`city` · `state` · `zip` · `marketing_opt_in` — and `email` was not one of them**, so it never entered
`supplied`, never entered `fields`, and could not enter the patch. 🔴 **The branch is why it stayed
invisible: the INSERT carries `email` as its OWN literal (`:241`), so a NEW customer always got theirs and
only a REPEAT customer lost it** — the path that fires on the second visit. _Measured on customer
`0ee368fe` (Diane Foster): `email` `''` after a checkout that typed one and sent the invoice to it;
`updated_at` the same second as the order; `billing_line1/city/zip` filled from the SAME payload (offered);
`address_line1` correctly NOT clobbered (offered, stored non-blank — rule (b) working)._

✅ **THE FIX — one `offer()` line plus a NAMED divergence.** `offer('email', customer.email)` puts email
through the same trim-and-omit-if-blank gate as every other field. A new **`SUPPLIED_WINS = ['email']`**
branch in the patch loop makes a typed email REPLACE the stored one. ⚠️ **THE NO-BLANKING GUARANTEE RESTS ON
`offer()`, NOT ON THAT BRANCH:** a blank/whitespace/null email fails `given()` and never reaches `fields`,
so "supplied wins" can only ever be reached by a value someone actually typed — **omission, never a null
write** (A9). The constant is NAMED rather than achieved by email's absence from `FILLABLE` (which would
already have produced the behaviour, and which the next person to extend that list would have silently
reverted).

⚠️ **A STATED DIVERGENCE (§6 r10): `email` is the ONE field here that does not follow fill-never-clobber.**
Rule (b) protects a curated value from a hurried counter capture; email is the opposite case — the register
is where a customer corrects it. **`phone` is NOT the same defect and never was** — it was already offered
and IS in `FILLABLE`, so it fills a blank and refuses to overwrite a curated value. Both rules are asserted
on the SAME payload (probes C1/C2/C2b).

✅ **R-12 — the write proves it wrote:** the fill-update's count check went `updRows?.length === 0` →
**`!== 1`**, refusing the zero-row PostgREST shape (what an RLS refusal looks like) AND a write that hit more
rows than the single row it named. 🔴 **The cap did not recognise the ruling's own shape and FAILED the
build for adopting it** — `scripts/verify-zero-row-writes.mjs`'s CHECKED regex knew `length === 0` / `< 1` /
`!x?.length` / `> 0` and not `!== 1`. Widened to the exact-count form + probes **Z3b/Z3c/Z3d** (the third a
negative control). ✏️ **That blind spot had been manufacturing false debt: the widening moved THREE
`scripts/lib/memberSession.mjs` sites OUT of the unchecked baseline (82 → 79, zero added) — all three already
written as `(data ?? []).length !== 1` before R-12 existed.**

**PROOF** — `customerUpsert.test.ts` (NEW, 34 probes). **Every probe reads `db.rows.customers[0]`, never the
return value**: the function reports WHICH ROW, never WHAT LANDED IN IT, so a build that dropped the field
again would return an identical and entirely truthful result. Four promised cases (new email stored · edited
email replaces · blank leaves the stored value untouched, in all four absent shapes · no email either side,
no crash) plus the insert half, the org branch, trimming, and R-12 both directions with a negative control.
**Red-first ×4 against the real source (STD-024)** — offer line deleted (5 fail) · email demoted to FILLABLE
(2 fail: the *edited* case a fill-only fix would have passed) · count check weakened to `=== 0` (1 fail: the
two-row probe) · blank email admitted to the payload (5 fail: the fix becoming a worse defect). Source
`diff`-restored identical after each.

**BAR: BUILDER-COMPLETE.** `npm run verify` exit 0, ZERO NET-NEW (5 / 247 / 10 / 12 / 15) · 33/33 test files ·
1240 assertions · api/ **12/12** · **NO schema, NO migration, NO policy, NO new permission string, NO new
api/ fn.** **OWNER-PROOF OWED** — owner-test cards 9 · 10 · 11 (`docs/owner-tests/customer-edit-surface-full-surface-test.md`),
all `owed`; **card 10 is the one that protects the fix** (a blank email must not blank a stored one).
`[TRACE:PERSON]` ON.

⚠️ **KNOWN AND FILED, NOT FIXED:** `customers.email` and `people.email` can now disagree — the spine never
backfills an email onto a person it matched by phone (`personUpsert.ts:87-99` only READS by email).
**tech-debt #112.** The OCR customer-creation path, all matching/dedup logic, and the `billing_*` vs
`address_*` split were scoped OUT and are untouched.


## QBO Customer Identity Resolution — the three-way rule (D-47) — added 2026-07-16

**What:** How a TRACE customer resolves to a QuickBooks customer on the invoice push. Replaces an
email-only match that cross-billed **nine real invoices to the wrong person over two months**.
**Status:** 🟡 BUILDER-COMPLETE — owner-proof owed (ledger #130) | **Vertical:** shared + cultivar | **Type:** correctness / integration
**Location:** `packages/shared/src/quickbooks/customerIdentity.ts` (the ONE decision fn — pure, 22 tests) ·
`packages/shared/src/utils/personName.ts` (person-name canonical key) ·
`packages/cultivar-os/api/qbo/invoice/cultivar.ts` (the IO + stored-link verification)

**The rule — two independent fields must CONCUR before TRACE binds an identity:**

| email match | name match | action |
|---|---|---|
| yes | yes | **LINK** |
| no | no | **CREATE** |
| **YES** | **NO** | **CREATE** ← the Terrence case; never link |
| no | YES | **SURFACE** (409, owner resolves) |

**Root asymmetry (the lesson):** QBO enforces `DisplayName` **UNIQUE**; QBO does **NOT** enforce
unique email (families and offices share one). TRACE matched on the one field QBO permits to
collide and ignored the one it guarantees. → **Match on the field the external system guarantees
unique.** → ✅ **STD-019** (ACTIVATED 2026-07-16, STANDARDS.md v2.4) — generalizes to any external
identity binding (Stripe, supplier catalogs), not just QBO.

**Also:** a stored `qb_customer_id` is a **CACHE, not a fact** — verified against the live QBO
record before billing (name drift → refuse + flag; this is the check that would have caught the bug
on invoice #1 instead of #9). Collision guard (two TRACE customers may never share one
`qb_customer_id`, business_id-scoped). QBO 6240 duplicate-DisplayName **surfaces**; the silent
email-as-DisplayName junk-record fallback is **retired**. Closes tech-debt #53.

**No readback exists** (recon 2026-07-16): nothing imports QBO customers into `customers`, so the
matcher is load-bearing — without it any party predating TRACE 6240s on every push. The three
readback-looking artifacts (`pullQBOCustomers`, `ExternalBridge.qbo.pullCustomers`,
`QuickBooksConnector.jsx`) are dead Railway-era code hitting a deleted endpoint.

**⚠️ Reuse note:** does **NOT** use the #61 `nameTokenSet` — that key splits on apostrophes
(`O'Brien`→`{brien}` ≠ `OBrien`→`{obrien}`) because it drops 1-char tokens for the botanical hybrid
`x`. `personName.ts` elides apostrophes and reuses `tokenSetsEqual`. **The same blind spot is a
LATENT bug in the plant resolver** (slug `baileys-red-twig-dogwood` won't match `Bailey's Red Twig
Dogwood`) — flagged, not fixed.

**Decision:** `docs/decisions/2026-07-16-qbo-customer-identity-resolution-D47.md`

---

## QB Token Refresh

**What:** Proactive QuickBooks token refresh. Checks expiry before any invoice call. Refreshes if missing or within 10 min of expiry. Sets `qb_needs_reconnect=true` and returns null if refresh token is dead.  
**Status:** ✅ Built — TypeScript  
**Vertical:** shared | **Type:** infrastructure  
**Location:** `packages/shared/src/quickbooks/refresh.ts`  
**Used by:** `packages/cultivar-os/api/qbo/invoice/cultivar.ts`

**⚠️ QB integration scope: receivables only (Audit 6, 2026-06-06).** The current QB integration (`api/qbo/`) covers invoices (money IN) and customer lookup/create. Payables (money OUT — expense write, Attachable image archive, Chart of Accounts query, Purchase/Bill write) are confirmed API-capable but NOT yet wired in TRACE code. Payables wire-up = Receipt Keeper v2 scope. See `docs/audits/2026-06-06-audits-5-6-quickbooks.md` §6b.

**✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-08 — proactive expiry surfacing live.** `qbo/status.ts` now checks `accounting_token_expires_at` on every dashboard load; attempts silent `refreshQBToken()` if expired; returns `needsReconnect: true` if refresh fails. `Dashboard.tsx` derives the banner immediately from `accounting_token_expires_at` client-side and applies the authoritative server result from `checkQbStatus()`. A dead/expired connection now announces itself on page load — no mid-use 401 required. STD-007 added as the class-of-bug record.

**✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-19 — QB routes 500 root-caused + fixed (commit `14a9a82`, tech-debt #34).** Both `/api/qbo/auth-url` and `/api/qbo/status` returned `FUNCTION_INVOCATION_FAILED`. Root cause: `packages/cultivar-os/api/qbo/router.ts:15` imported `refreshQBToken` from `../../../../shared/...` (FOUR `../` → nonexistent `<repo-root>/shared/`); router.ts sits at `api/qbo/` so the correct depth is THREE `../` (now `../../../shared/src/quickbooks/refresh` — confirmed `router.ts:15`). The unresolved import crashed the function at module load → 500 on every qbo route regardless of token state. Proven via esbuild (old path fails resolution, new bundles clean). Connect flow now reaches the auth-url link instead of 500.

## Social Media Module (shared + Cultivar OS surface)

**What:** Period-based AI social post generator. Aggregates the week's sales into platform-specific captions. Owner edits, copies to clipboard, posts manually.  
**Status:** ✅ Generator moved to shared 2026-06-08 — business_type-parameterized, Sonnet via gateway. Table de-nouned (AC-1). Edit/save widget live.  
**Vertical:** shared (generator) + cultivar (surface) | **Type:** tile  

**Backend:**
- `packages/shared/src/social/generate.ts` — `generateSocialDrafts()` — business_type-parameterized, Sonnet via AI gateway. `BUSINESS_DESCRIPTORS` map: variation is data, not code. `[TRACE:socialdraft]` behind `SOCIALDRAFT_DEBUG`. **🔴 KNOWN HOLE, UNFIXED (2026-08-23, ledger #197):** this is the SECOND generator, and its `systemPrompt` (`:47-52`) asks for *"Write warmly and **specifically** — reference what actually happened"* **with no anti-fabrication clause**, while the campaign generator now has one. **The absence is conspicuous rather than accidental: this prompt already carries THREE prohibitions** (*"Never mention prices. Never include customer last names, emails, or contact info."*) — it is demonstrably a place where prohibitions live. **There is no single place that would have fixed both: `executeCapability` passes `opts.system` straight through (`ai/execute.ts:26-29`) — no shared preamble seam exists.** Left unfixed because the session's scope was ONE app-code change and this prompt has a different shape (a list, not prose), so the wording is a judgement to make deliberately. Recorded as **CARD 2 `needs-test`** on `docs/owner-tests/social-campaign-full-surface-test.md`.
- `packages/cultivar-os/api/social/generate-posts.ts` — thin Vercel handler: reads context (orders, plants, config) → calls shared generator → inserts to `social_drafts` with `subject_type='inventory'`, `subject_id=null`.
- `packages/cultivar-os/api/social/enable.ts` — upserts `business_modules` with `{ advert_channels: AdvertChannel[], cadence }`. No blotato_account_id. `advert_channels` is the single source of truth for all channel config (social + SMS).

**social_drafts table (de-nouned 2026-06-08):**
- Columns: `id, business_id, platform, original_text, edited_text, status, subject_type, subject_id, cadence, period_start, period_end, copied_at, post_type, created_at`
- `original_text`: immutable AI proposal. `edited_text`: what owner saved (null until edited).
- `subject_type`/`subject_id`: AC-1 compliant subject ref (value, not column name). Cultivar writes `subject_type='inventory'`, `subject_id=null` for period aggregates.
- Status lifecycle: `draft → edited → approved → copied` (+ `copied_at` timestamp).
- **No** `content`, `order_id`, `plant_id` (all retired 2026-06-08).
- **`social_drafts_platform_check` (2026-06-09):** CHECK constraint now migration-controlled. Hand-applied pre-migration-era; original allowed list was `(instagram, facebook, tiktok, twitter)`. Extended to include `'sms'` by `supabase/migrations/20260609_social_drafts_platform_check.sql`. ⚠️ Migration pending David apply + verify. Without this: SMS-enabled generation runs write zero rows (atomic batch rolls back when sms row violates constraint).

**Dashboard edit/save widget:**
- Generate button → calls `/api/social/generate-posts` → inserts drafts → `loadSocialDrafts()` reloads.
- Draft card: editable textarea (original_text); on blur → `handleSaveEdit()` → writes `edited_text + status='edited'`.
- [Copy] button → copies to clipboard + `status='copied' + copied_at` → removes from queue.
- [Download image] — stub, disabled (seam declared below).
- [Open platform] — links to platform URL.
- Edited drafts show green border + "✓ Edited" chip.

**Voice-delta captured (seam declared):**  
`original_text` vs `edited_text` = the training delta, accumulating now, no consumer yet.  
**remaining:** voice-learning BI — query original vs edited pairs to detect voice patterns. Horizon: v2/later. NOT a defect — data is flowing cleanly; the analysis surface is the unfilled seam.

**Aggregation/cadence generator (seam declared):**  
`cadence` is stored per module config; `period_start`/`period_end` captured per draft. The generator that reads `config.cadence` and auto-triggers a new generation window (preventing per-purchase overwhelm) is the next step.  
**remaining:** cadence-triggered generation — scheduler that fires generate-posts on cadence rhythm. Horizon: Social Rhythm (next social session). NOT a defect — the data model is ready; the trigger is the unfilled seam.

**advert_channels config (2026-06-08):**
`business_modules.config.advert_channels: [{type:'social'|'sms', name:string, enabled:boolean}]` — flat typed list. `generate-posts.ts` reads this and generates ONLY for enabled channels. SMS is a separate entry (`type:'sms'`, always one post, no image prompt). LEXICON RULE: "platform" is reserved for the top-level TRACE substrate; "channel"/"advert_channel" is used inside the product.

**Not built:** image generation. Direct social publishing — Blotato removed 2026-06-08 (misrepresented capability).

**Hidden seams declared (inert, demand-gated, priced — see `api/campaigns.ts` preamble):**
- `auto-publish seam` — wire a vetted publisher adapter at activation. No refactor needed. Gate: demand + pricing.
- `sms-auto-send seam` — TCPA/10DLC/opt-out compliance is real work at activation; adopt provider model. Gate: demand + pricing + provider selection.

## QR Checkout Flow (Cultivar OS)

**What:** Complete scan-to-invoice flow. QR scan → plant profile → add-ons → customer capture → cart review → order submit → QB invoice → email confirmation. **Now MULTI-ITEM** (2026-07-08): scan many items into ONE order, services attach by rule, owner adjusts before submit.  
**Order roster + CRUD (2026-07-08, ledger #100 — BUILDER-COMPLETE, owner-proof owed):** the `/orders` roster names every order by the **D-34 dual anchor** (specimen `cultivar_plants` wins, else stock-line `business_inventory.name`) — closes the "Unknown plant" gap on scan/stock-line orders (`lib/orderItemName.ts`, reused roster + drill-in). NEW `/orders/:id` drill-in (`pages/OrderDetail.tsx`) shows ALL line items + ALL services (`order_service_selections`, previously written-but-never-shown) + customer + delivery date + status + totals. **Owner/manager CRUD** (`api/orders/submit.ts` action=update|delete|status, token-gated: owner by `owner_id` OR `manage_orders` perm; staff READ-ONLY, server-enforced): edit line qty + delivery date, remove lines, change status (`pending→confirmed→fulfilled→cancelled`, `lib/orderStatus.ts`), delete — each SERVER-RECOMPUTED (re-read `sell_price`, re-net services) with inventory RE-RESERVED (release old/reserve new) / RELEASED on delete. QB-invoice staleness surfaced on edit (R-QBSTALE). NEW shared server-gate `packages/shared/src/auth/callerPermission.ts` (`callerHoldsPermission` moved from ingest.ts + `callerIsBusinessOwner`). ZERO schema, ZERO new api-fn (12/12).

**SELECT-POLICY COVERAGE CAP — EVERY TABLE HAS RLS *AND* A POLICY THAT CAN ACTUALLY SELECT (2026-08-01, ledger #178).** NEW `scripts/verify-select-policies.mjs`, wired into `npm run verify` between universals and write-paths. **The rule:** every live table in the migration corpus must have RLS ENABLED and ≥1 SELECT-capable policy — `FOR SELECT`, `FOR ALL`, or **no `FOR` clause** (Postgres defaults to ALL, encoded not assumed) — **or be DECLARED in `select-policy-declarations.json` with a reason.** RLS-on-with-no-policy is DENY-ALL: fail-closed, never a leak, and **invisible** — the surface comes up empty with no error anywhere, which is precisely how it bit three times (`modules` + `nursery_modules` 2026-05-22, `orders` 2026-05-27). Deny-all is legitimate for server-only reference data; it is not a legitimate ACCIDENT, and **the declaration is what tells those two apart.** **Tables are DERIVED from the corpus — never a hardcoded list.** 🔴 **The declaration list asserts itself in the other direction:** a declaration for a table that no longer exists, or that has since GAINED a select-capable policy, is **STALE and FAILS THE BUILD** — because `verify-universals`' `OWNER_ONLY_PENDING` is a hardcoded gap list with **6 of 9 entries now stale** printing on every run, which is how a gap list stops being read (tech-debt #73). **17 probes both directions (STD-022):** the three real defect shapes (no policies at all · policies exist but none can select · the only SELECT policy dropped later), RLS-enabled-then-disabled, plus the negatives that stop it rubber-stamping (`FOR ALL` counts · a bare policy counts · a DROPPED table is not evaluated · a still-earned declaration is not stale). **RED-FIRST against the REAL corpus, not a fixture: exit 1, `37 live tables · 35 covered · 2 UNDECLARED`** — `platform_config` and `permission_aliases` — each then verified deliberate against **its own migration's stated words** plus a grep for client readers (`platform_config`: one reader, `ocr.ts:170`, service-key; `permission_aliases`: zero, and the migration REVOKEs ALL) before being declared. **🔴 A self-caught defect before it was trusted:** the first draft reported three tables named `at`, `migration` and `only` — all prose inside `--` comments; *a detector that reads prose as schema invents tables.* Comment-stripping added; probe S8 is permanent. **STATED LIMITS, because a cap that overreaches is worse than one that says what it cannot see:** it reads the REPO corpus not the live catalog (a hand-made dashboard policy is invisible — which is why §6 r17 exists), and it does **not** evaluate `TO <role>` or the USING predicate — *"a select-capable policy exists"* is not *"the right people can read."* **Closes CLAUDE.md Open Architecture Decision #11**, whose own trigger (*"before any new table is added"*) fired at least three times with nothing behind it.

**TECH-DEBT #78 CLOSED — THE RATCHET BASELINES NO LONGER KEY ON LINE NUMBERS (2026-08-01, ledger #177).** `verify-zero-row-writes` and `verify-field-lists` keyed on `path:line`, so any edit that shifted a line reported the same site as NEW — it fired **three times in one day** on 2026-07-31. **David's ruling is the reason it counts as a defect:** *a ratchet that cries wolf teaches reflexive re-baselining, and a reflexive re-baseline is how a real violation gets absorbed.* NEW shared **`scripts/lib/siteKey.mjs`** (ONE implementation, both caps): **`path::binding#table.verb`** + an `@n` ordinal only within an identical triple — the shape `authority-grants-baseline.json` already used. **Measured before choosing: all 167 candidate sites resolve a binding, ZERO failures — so no line-number fallback exists anywhere**; the single stated fallback is `?` for a table out of window (1 site of 120). **COUNTS PROVEN IDENTICAL BEFORE RE-LOCKING** via a new permanent `--dump` mode: **85 → 85** and **35 → 35**, distinct keys, **0 unmatched in either direction** (the prompt's 81/37 corrected to 85/35). **8 new probes both directions**, incl. *a genuinely new site still fails* and *renaming the enclosing function DOES re-key it*. **Live proof:** the exact comment insertion that broke both caps twice yesterday now reports RATCHET CLEAN. **🔴 Two self-caught defects in the fix:** 38 of 120 first-run keys read `::if#`/`::for#` (control-flow keywords matched the bare-method pattern — line-stable but unreadable, and *a key a human cannot read gets re-baselined without being read*); and field-lists matched ON `.from('x')` so its backward window resolved `?` for every table, caught by three probes going 0-for-3.

**BUILD 2 — THE TILE PATH (2026-07-31, ledger #176).** **THE DEFECT:** `useModules.ts:88` filtered `.filter(t => can(t.required_permission))` and read `status === 'planned'` AFTER it — so **a planned tile rendered only for a session that already held its string.** `planned` was reachable exactly for the people who did not need telling. Fixed to `t.status === 'planned' || can(...)`; the gate is unchanged for everything else and a planned tile exposes nothing (no route, no data, not clickable). **THE SECOND HALF:** `dashboardTiles()` filters `kind !== 'readout'`, so `business_insights` **never entered `useModules` at all** — and `Dashboard.tsx` had **no render block for it whatsoever**. It carried `status:'planned'` from registration and rendered NOTHING to anyone, ever. A `readoutPlanned()` branch now renders **`<BeingBuilt>`, MOUNTED FOR THE FIRST TIME** (written in Phase 3 / #171, unused since — the reason card 12 was `needs-test`). **NEW `TileState` `'planned'`** in the shared `Tile.tsx`: amber **SOON** badge, own colour at reduced strength, inert like `locked` so every interaction guard reads both — **deliberately NOT the red lock**, because `useModules` used to map planned → `locked` and *collapsing them makes a roadmap item look like a snub and a snub look like a roadmap item*. (Note what that reveals: until now the ONLY tiles ever rendering a red lock were the unbuilt ones, since unheld tiles were filtered out entirely.) **capA ASSERTION 6, ONE DIRECTION:** a tile on a `planned` permission must itself be `status:'planned'`; **the converse is NOT asserted** — 7 of the 8 planned tiles gate on LIVE strings and the cap prints them every run so the exemption is visible. 7 probes; **STD-024 red-first against the REAL registry** (flipping `business_insights` to `live` → reported by name, exit 1). **ONE SOURCE PER SURFACE, mechanically:** the readout branch reads `tileByKey(key)?.status`, never the manifest — Roles page reads the manifest, dashboard reads the tile, and `maintenance:override` (a planned permission with NO tile) is why they must stay separate. **RULED: `reports:read` stays in `financial`**, with the judgement flag left in the code. Owner-test cards 19–20 (**20 is runnable ONLY as STAFF**) and card 12 flipped `needs-test` → `owed`.

**A RUNNING TRIAL MEANS THE MODULE IS LIVE (2026-08-02, ledger #182).** The 08-01 seed spec mapped `enabled = (billing === 'core')`, so all seven paid add-ons seeded **DARK with a thirty-day clock running over them** — nothing was being trialled. 🔴 **`enabled:false` is a FUNCTIONAL GATE, not a label** (`api/social/generate-posts.ts:52` refuses on it), so social_media counted toward a $19/mo conversion **while the server refused to generate a single post**. **David's model: the site goes live, EVERYTHING WORKS, and at the end of the term the unpaid modules go behind the fuzz — the clock ENDS access, it never withholds it.** **SHIPPED:** `moduleSeedRow` now derives liveness from the CLOCK, not the billing class (`isCore || startTrial`), so an add-on with `trial_days: 0` still seeds dark · **`configured` flips with `enabled`** because `useModules` renders `active` only on both — correcting `enabled` alone fixes the data and leaves `[ENABLE]` on screen · NEW **`trialClock.ts`**, the ONE reader of the stored `(trial_started_at, trial_days)` pair, in `shared` because the marketplace reads the same number and two surfaces disagreeing about a customer's days left is a disagreement about money; **`null` (no clock) and `0` (lapsed) are deliberately different answers** (D-9) · `useModules` READS `config`, which it had been SELECTING and dropping since 08-01 — **invisible to tsc AND knip**, because a field in the select string and in the interface is 'used' everywhere except where it matters · the countdown is an **ANNOTATION on an ACTIVE tile, NOT a new `TileState`** · **`20260802_trialling_modules_are_live.sql` GATED** — `20260801c`'s `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (correct, unchanged) means the code fix reaches every FUTURE tenant and cannot touch the 11 rows already seeded; its scope carries a **lapsed-term guard that is a no-op today and written anyway**, because a repair must not be able to grant access a term no longer covers. **THE GUARD WAS THE DEFECT:** `moduleSeed.test.ts` B3 ASSERTED the bug — 138 assertions green because the test and the mapping were the same mistake written twice. B3 inverted, **B6 rewritten not deleted** (the danger is live-with-nothing-that-ends-it), **B6b** (liveness has two sources and no third), A7b (fails anyone who summarises this as *'add-ons are now enabled'*); 135 → **151**, red-first ×16. NEW `trialClock.test.ts` 16 probes both directions, **T16 a negative control that fails a null-returning stub**. ⚠️ **NOT BUILT: expiry** — nothing flips a lapsed trial off, so it keeps working; that is the fuzz, filed. ⚠️ **RULING OWED: `unpriced`** — `cost_to_produce` and `inventory_intake` are BUILT AND WORKING, have no price, get no clock, so they render `[ENABLE]` forever on a purchase nobody can make.

**THE FOURTH PERMISSION STATUS — `planned` (2026-07-31, ledger #175, BUILD 1 of 2).** `declared-unwired` conflated *"nothing will ever enforce this"* with *"scoped for a planned feature"*, and the model contradicted itself in one conversation: the six-state ruling renders BEING BUILT with a "coming soon" hover, while §7.1 FILTERED the same string out of the Roles catalog. **David's resolution: the pill is real, the feature is not built — and that is not a fake pill.** A fake pill claims something WORKS when it does not; a planned pill says a thing is COMING, which is true, and it is the conversation starter. **SHIPPED:** `PermissionStatus` gains `planned` (Option A — a fourth status, NOT a `roadmap` second axis, because every consumer is status-keyed) · **`reports:read` MINTED** (it was gating `business_insights` without existing in ANY manifest — #88's actual defect) · `maintenance:override` → planned (the PMI block is deliberate and unbuilt) · `deliveries.route:update` → planned (its note already read as a roadmap item) · NEW `PLANNED_PERMISSIONS` + `UNGRANTABLE_PERMISSIONS` (= declared-unwired ∪ planned) · `CATALOG_PERMISSIONS` renders planned, `HIDDEN_PERMISSIONS` deliberately does not hide it · `OWNER_LOCKED_SET` excludes it · dashed-amber **non-interactive** chip (`🚧 … · coming soon`) with `onPlannedSelect` declared as the seam for the **requirement hook, which is NOT built** · `TeamConsole.tsx:56-58`'s comment corrected in the same commit — it argued the superseded position in prose. **THE FLOOR: `planned` requires a build behind it** (a named gap, card, slot or decision); `campaigns:create` stays declared-unwired because the next verb being obvious is not a scoped build, and `team:create/update/delete` stay because they are a deliberate architectural NO — *"never", not "unbuilt"* (removal is #90). **capQ** extended to the union, 19 probes; its applied-migration trap solved by removing the `manifest ⊆ R-B2` direction **after testing (not assuming) that a derivable replacement was false — only 3 of 8 R-B2 members are alias-reachable**. **🔴 The test suite caught `OWNER_LOCKED_SET` about to hand the owner three strings for features that do not exist** (and force a 52 → 55 edit to an applied migration). `permissionManifest.test.ts` 74 assertions, 9 new. **BUILD 2 — THE TILE PATH — NOT STARTED (#89):** `dashboardTiles()` excludes readouts and `useModules` filters `can()` BEFORE reading `status`, so `planned` is reachable only for tiles gated on strings people already hold. Owner-test card 18.

**capA ASSERTION 5 — THE A7 CLIENT-GATE SWEEP, MECHANICAL (2026-07-31, ledger #174).** Every literal permission string in `packages/*/src` that is not a current model entry FAILS the build — capP's assertion 1 pointed at the layer it had never read. **capP structurally could not catch these:** its retired-string scan (P15) reads the ROUTER and RLS-checked strings for `fate:'retire'`, and `view_costs` is `'split'` while `import_pricing` is `'rename'`. **THE COUNT: 4 distinct strings · 5 sites.** **2 LIVE, both fixed:** `Dashboard.tsx:91` `view_costs` → **`costs:read`** (admitted NOBODY including the OWNER — the inventory-value metric was dark and `:160` did not request `unit_cost`; the SELECT was never deleted, it was gated on the same dead flag) and `InventoryImport.tsx:71` `import_pricing` → **`inventory:import_price`** (told the owner his own prices would not save; its comment still described the short-circuit Phase 2 deleted). **2 LATENT, DECLARED awaiting rulings #87/#88:** `view_dashboard` (navPermission's fallback — unreachable today, 0 of 26 nav nodes reach it) and `reports:read` (`business_insights`, `status:'planned'` — ships invisible). **12 probes both directions; STD-024 red-first on the current tree.** **🔴 Four self-caught cap errors before it reported a number (49 → 7 → 4):** an inline length bound that mis-paired quotes (caught by probe S3) · reading `PERMISSION_MANIFEST` (a derivation) instead of the `RESOURCES` seed table, which parsed ZERO permissions — the cap now THROWS on zero · singleton `'x:y'` entries with no `verbs` array, which would have flagged two live ENFORCED strings · line-level instead of argument-level position matching, which flagged a tile KEY colliding with a legacy name. **🔴 And one pre-existing defect in capA committed since 2026-07-30:** `decomment()` filled blanked literals with a NUL byte, making the file **BINARY to `grep`** — the cap guarding the authority model was invisible to text search. Fixed. **METHOD RULING (David): a string is derived from the ACT, never from who needs to pass.** Owner-test cards 16–17.

**THE STRING MOVED AGAIN THE SAME DAY, AND capA GREW AN ASSERTION TO MATCH (2026-07-31, ledger #173).** David's second ruling: **"may create an order" IMPLIES "may set its fields" — A PERMISSION GATES A CAPABILITY, NOT A FIELD.** `orders:update` was right for the wrong reason (it happened to include Lauren); the rule is that whoever may perform the act may set the act's data. **Final string: `orders:create`** — and **STAFF get the field**, because they hold `orders:create` and take orders; gating on `:update` would have handed it to the manager and withheld it from the person standing in the lot. **`submit.ts` needs NOTHING and tech-debt #84 is closed RULED-NOT-BUILT**, with the reasoning recorded in `CustomerCapture.tsx` itself so nobody adds a server gate later on the theory that its absence was an oversight; `order_discount:apply` / `tax_exempt:apply` stay separate strings because they are MONEY and TAX ACTS, not because they are fields. **capA ASSERTION 4 (NEW):** every authority site — `can('literal')` bindings, inline control renders, route `permission=` props — resolves to the ROLES that pass it against the three bundles; **41 sites** baselined in **NEW `authority-grants-baseline.json`**, keyed **`file::binding` not `file:line`** (tech-debt #78's class); a role-set change fails the build unless re-baselined **with a `why`**; **19 probes both directions** (a narrowing fails, **a widening fails**, a declared change passes, an empty grant set with no `why` fails on first sight); **STD-024 red-first against the REAL `123ad3a` file** — shape immaculate, caught as `grant-narrowed: OWNER+MANAGER+STAFF → OWNER`. New script `npm run authority:baseline`. **🔴 The first run found TWO live legacy-string gates admitting NOBODY** — `Dashboard.tsx::canViewCosts` (`view_costs`; the owner's own inventory-value metric is dark and the query does not SELECT `unit_cost`) and `InventoryImport.tsx::canImportPricing` (`import_pricing`) — the #170 class, instances 3 and 4, **DECLARED known-bad and printing red every run, not fixed and not silently accepted** (RULINGS OWED #85/#86). Stated blind spot, printed by the cap itself: **6 dynamic `can()` sites** and the three DEFAULT bundles rather than a tenant's edited arrays. Open: R-QBSTALE, R-STATUS. **Follow-up (2026-07-08):** the drill-in showed "PLANTS (0)" while the subtotal included plant cost — a PostgREST nested-embed drop (order_items embed under the `.maybeSingle()` orders query returned `[]` while the sibling services embed populated). FIXED: `order_items` fetched in its OWN top-level query (`ITEM_COLS`), anchor-agnostic, names via the same `orderItemName.ts` resolver; `[TRACE:ROSTER]` now logs line count + anchors + a subtotal reconciliation. Completes the drill-in. **Fix (2026-07-15, ledger #129, migration GATED):** the status-change write (`action=status`) was **500-ing** on `confirmed`/`fulfilled` — the live `orders.status` carried a pre-D-37 CHECK `CHECK (status = ANY (ARRAY['pending','invoiced','paid','cancelled']))` (a payment lifecycle) that rejected the code's fulfillment vocabulary; undetected because every order sat at `pending`. ONE gated migration `20260715_orders_status_drop_check.sql` DROPs the constraint (AC-1/STD-011/D-37 — `ORDER_STATUSES` in code is the canonical guard; handleStatus already validates → clean 400 on a bad value). NO app-code change. **R-STATUS blocking defect CLOSED — ✅ OWNER-PROVEN 2026-07-15** (David, live: migration applied + catalog-verified A–C; Confirmed/Fulfilled/Cancelled all persist with no 500; Cancelled restores stock per D-42; bad value still returns clean 400). Sibling `orders_transport_method_check` flagged as the same class (tech-debt #52).  
**SERVICE PRICE OVERRIDE IS A DISCOUNT — the QBO `6070` fix (2026-07-16, D-48 proposed, **committed + PUSHED `5f16c42`**, BUILDER-COMPLETE, owner-proof owed; ledger #131; ZERO migration, ZERO api-fn 12/12):** 🟡 **THE SCAR (live, CLV-20260716-1156):** the owner overrode Placement Service $1575→$1000 and the line was written `retailUnit 225 · qty 7 · retailTotal 1575 · discountAmt 0 · netTotal 1000` — **the line CONTRADICTED ITSELF** (1575 − 0 ≠ 1000). **QuickBooks was the FIRST thing anywhere downstream that multiplied rate × qty** and REJECTED the invoice: `6070 "Amount is not equal to UnitPrice * Qty. Supplied value:1,000"` (2 pushes, 16:57 + 17:19; the invoice does not exist in QBO). Silent because every TRACE surface rendered $1000 and reconciled cleanly ($2020 / 7.6% $153.52 / $2173.52 — all correct): **no TRACE surface checks that a LINE is internally consistent** — every check we had looked at *aggregates ACROSS* lines, and the defect lived *WITHIN* one. **ROOT:** D-43 SPECCED the invariant `retail_total − discount_amt === net_total` and verified it in a MIGRATION — which passed **only because no override existed yet**; nothing enforced it where lines are BORN. **An invariant that is only verified in a migration is not enforced.** **BUILT (David's ruling — an override IS a discount; supersedes D-39's "leakage ≠ discount" service branch):** (1) `computeOrderPricing`'s service branch PRESERVES the retail baseline and puts the concession in `discountAmt` 575 / `discountPct` 36.51 (= `round2(discountAmt/retailTotal × 100)`, convention stated) — the baseline is what makes the giveaway visible AND what lets QBO multiply and agree; (2) the invariant is **ENFORCED AT COMPUTE TIME and THROWS** (half-cent tolerance for float dust) — unfireable by construction, it guards the next branch that forgets; (3) an **UPWARD override is a NEGATIVE discount (surcharge) — ALLOWED** (refusing would REGRESS an ability the owner has today; stays consistent `1575 − (−425) = 2000`; pushes a positive "price adjusted" line; pct honest-negative, never rendered as "% off"); (4) **`discountTotal` restricted to GOODS-only + NEW `serviceAdjustmentTotal`** — the structural trap: `discountTotal`'s contract already SAID "goods only" but summed EVERY line, getting away with it *only because services were hardcoded to 0*; the $575 would have leaked into the "<tier> — N% off" line rendered against `goodsRetailSubtotal` (STD-011 — a tier discount and an owner concession are two DIFFERENT facts: different authority, label, audit trail); (5) **reason REQUIRED** (STD-013, reusing D-40's exemption shape) at UI (`Reason *` + surfaced error, no silent no-op) **and** server (`applyOverride` refuses + logs + charges the BASELINE — refusal charges MORE, the money-safe direction) **and** spine (so the gates agree by construction) — **NO MIGRATION: `override_reason` already existed on `order_service_selections` AND `order_items` (20260708:27,36), applied, and submit already WROTE it; it was simply never REQUIRED**; (6) **QBO** pushes the service at RETAIL (`225 × 7 = 1575` — internally consistent, accepted) + the 575 on the SAME negative-Amount mechanism as the tier discount via ONE extracted **`discountLine()`** helper (STD-011 — no forked second discount path), GATED so a normal order pushes byte-identical (zero regression), plus a NEW **reconcile check** that refuses to push when the lines don't sum to the order total (so a discount line can never double-count against an already-netted line); (7) **Confirmation (D-9)** — the type was `'success' | 'pending'`, so **there was no failed state to render**: a hard 400 rendered *"⏱ Invoice will sync shortly — Connect QuickBooks from the owner dashboard"*, wrong twice (QBO WAS connected, having minted customer 84 seconds earlier; and nothing would ever sync). Now THREE honest states `success | not_connected` (503 only) `| failed` (reason surfaced), scoped by `ownerView` — the owner gets the error + action, the customer a true neutral state with no internals and **no owner instruction** (which the old copy handed to customers on the public QR path). **⚠️ CAUGHT DURING THE BUILD:** `CartReview` fed `overrideTotal` WITHOUT `overrideReason` → Review would have shown $1575 while submit charged $1000 — the exact STD-012 divergence, re-created by the fix for a different bug; fixed same pass. **RECORDED:** `tierPricing.test.ts` test 16 asserted `discountAmt === 0` — **the defect was TESTED IN and blessed**; the suite asked whether the override replaced the charge (it did), never whether the line still added up (it didn't). Rewritten to assert the invariant, with that history in the file. **This fix MOVES NO MONEY** ($2173.52 to the penny). `[TRACE:PRICE]`/`[TRACE:LEAKAGE]`/`[TRACE:QBO]` ON (each line logs `invariantOk`). `npm run verify` exit 0 zero net-new; **138/138** tier-pricing tests (was 84; +54, RED-first per STD-002). **Flagged (NOT built):** `apply_discount` is DECLARED but not the gate (rides `manage_orders` + the server token gate — documented-with-reason); goods overrides don't exist yet (service-only by contract → **no stacking question today**), but when a plant-line override is built it WILL be one (the tier discount already occupies goods' `discountAmt`) — David decides then. Home: `docs/decisions/2026-07-16-service-price-override-is-a-discount-D48.md`.
**INVENTORY DECREMENT-ON-PAID — the missing per-unit stock depletion (2026-07-13, D-42 proposed, BUILDER-COMPLETE, migration gated, owner-proof owed; ledger #120):** 🟡 RECON verdict: a qty decrement NEVER existed — the order path only flipped a whole lot's `status` to `'reserved'` on ANY sale (submit.ts §11), which under D-34 (lot=SKU, qty=count) wrongly reserved all 45 of a 45-lot when 1 sold + hid the lot from the status-filtered dashboard `available_count` (the vanishing that *looked* like depletion). **MODEL (David, Amazon standard):** decrement `qty` PER-UNIT at ORDER-PAID/CONFIRMED (checkout complete + invoice generated — the §11 point), NOT at delivery (delivery is a later event that does NOT touch qty). **BUILT:** (1) ONE atomic RPC **`adjust_inventory_qty(p_lot_id, p_business_id, p_delta)`** — a single guarded UPDATE (`qty + delta >= 0` = the oversell guard), concurrency-safe (NO JS read-modify-write, so two concurrent orders on one lot can't lose a unit), status DERIVES from the new qty (qty>0→'available', qty<=0→'depleted' — the existing datasheet vocabulary; a manual 'damaged'/'returned' is preserved), `SECURITY DEFINER`/`search_path=''`, `service_role`-only EXECUTE (the RPC trusts its business_id arg → server-only, never `authenticated`). (2) §11 decrements `−quantity` targeting the RECONCILED anchor `rl.stockLineId ?? rl.plant.inventory_id` (was `plant.inventory_id`-only → could silently no-op on a stock-line line). (3) whole order lifecycle coherent through the SAME signed-delta RPC (STD-011/STD-012): **edit** `±(oldQty−newQty)` per lot, **delete/cancel** restore `+n` (guarded vs double-restore on an already-cancelled order). (4) OVERSELL surfaced never silent (D-9): a pre-flight `INSUFFICIENT_STOCK` 400 refuses the whole order before any write + the §11 RPC guard covers the concurrent-depletion race — qty never driven negative. (5) `reorder_point int` stub column (nullable, additive) for the NEXT build (reorder threshold). Old `setLotStatus` whole-lot-flip helper removed. **ONE additive gated migration `20260713_inventory_decrement_and_reorder.sql`** (column + RPC — David applies as postgres). **ZERO new api-fn** (the RPC is a Postgres function, NOT a Vercel fn — 12/12 held). `[TRACE:INVENTORY]` ON. `npm run verify` exit 0 zero net-new. **UNBLOCKS reconciliation** (counted vs expected = last count − sold) — was gated on real depletion. Home: `docs/decisions/2026-07-13-inventory-decrement-on-paid-D42.md`. Supersedes the whole-lot 'reserved' reservation model.

**↑ SUPERSEDED IN PART by D-52 (2026-07-21, ledger #144 — **🟢 OWNER-PROVEN 2026-07-22**, SHA `e7471df`).** **THE ORDER LOOP IS PROVEN IN BOTH SHAPES** (David, live, tenant `f7ec5d67`): **DELIVERY** (order `897be269`) wrote `order_created` + `order_committed` at **16:49:15** with **delta 0** — on-hand UNTOUCHED and **no `sale` event at all** — then `order_fulfilled` + `sale −6` at **16:53:34**, the two at the same instant and **four minutes after checkout**. **WALK-IN** (self transport) wrote `sale −2` at **17:06:50 AT CHECKOUT**, commit and fulfill collapsed into one instant with no separate fulfill step. **One model, two spacings.** Oversell refused against AVAILABLE at checkout review, naming both figures (*"0 available (29 on hand, 57 committed)"*). The reconcile surface then READ that sale correctly — evidence strip *"6 sold — 1 order"*, and at counted 38 vs book 40 only the **−2** residual surfaced for attribution, so the sales-netting math works on real events. QBO invoices generated (**#437** delivery, **#438** walk-in) and the netting warning fired on self-pickup (Texas Transp. Code Ch. 725) — **the Regina anchor, live.** ⚠️ **4 of the 8 owner-test cards remain `owed` and were deliberately NOT flipped:** *cancel-invents-no-stock* (the sharpest — under the moved decrement a wrong cancel-restore MANUFACTURES plants into an append-only log), the grid three-number lock popover, the dashboard wording, and the pre-D-52 REMEDIATION report.  D-42's *mechanism* all STANDS — the atomic `adjust_inventory_qty` RPC, the oversell-never-negative guard, status-derives-from-qty. Only its **TRIGGER POINT** moves: on-hand no longer decrements at CHECKOUT, it decrements at **FULFILLMENT** (`handleStatus`'s new `fulfilled` branch), because the plant is physically still in the yard until it is delivered or driven away. Checkout instead emits `order_committed` and the units become **COMMITTED** — derived from the open order, never stored (`src/lib/inventoryStates.ts`). **A WALK-IN (`transport_method === 'self'`) is born `fulfilled` and still decrements at checkout**, because commit and fulfill are the same instant when the customer drives away — and leaving it `pending` would count units as committed that are already on a trailer. **Oversell now checks AVAILABLE** (on-hand − committed − claimed-earlier-in-this-cart), which is the reservation protection D-42 structurally could not offer: under checkout-decrement, on-hand and available were the same number. **⚠️ The build's real finding: FOUR seams assumed checkout-decrement, not the two the spec named.** The cancel-restore, delete-restore and edit-delta arithmetic were each written believing checkout had already taken the stock; moving the decrement without moving those beliefs would have credited back a decrement that never happened — cancelling a pending order would have MANUFACTURED plants, recorded as unretractable fact in D-50's append-only ledger. All four now key off ONE predicate, `movesOnHand`. Grid gains locked **Committed/Available** columns (§6 r13 — locked WITH a reason); dashboard + `api/dashboard.ts` stop calling on-hand "available" (`on_hand_count`/`committed_count` added). **One-time remediation** `scripts/d52-remediate-committed-stock.mjs` credits back the pre-D-52 open orders as LEDGERED events — **report-only by default; it EXCLUDES walk-ins**, whose stock genuinely left. **ZERO api-fn (12/12), ZERO schema, ZERO migration.** Home: `docs/decisions/2026-07-21-inventory-states-onhand-committed-available-D52.md`.

The `:116` comment is CORRECTED not deleted — *"entering the date is what creates the delivery"* is true on the ReceiptKeeper path and false on this one. ⚠️ **This gate is CLIENT-ONLY** — `submit.ts` proves authority for discount/tax_exempt/order-edit/order-delete and **nothing for `delivery_date`** (tech-debt #84, deferred by David: demo is days away, `submit.ts` is the money path). 🔴 **Caught by owner-test CARD 6's FAIL clause on a manager walk — the first time that clause fired, on the site it was written about. Now multi-item, scan-driven, with a netting engine + adjustable itemization. **NO new schema, NO new `api/` file** — the per-order/per-plant attach rule already lived on `service_offerings.price_type`/`price_unit` (recon R1–R5), and the order write rides the existing `submit.ts` (12/12 held).
