## Discovery Module (Cultivar OS — v0)

**What:** Silent partner analysis engine. Fetches a prospect's website, runs a two-pass AI analysis (fast identity extraction + deep profile), and generates a "silent partner" email: what the business is doing well, what could amplify it. David sends the analysis to the prospect; they receive something real and specific regardless of whether they ever sign up.  
**Status:** ✅ v0 built — TypeScript (2026-06-05). See DISCOVERY_MODULE_BRIEF.md for full spec.  
**Vertical:** cultivar (admin surface today; cross-vertical engine) | **Type:** capability  
**Location:** `packages/shared/src/discovery/` + `packages/cultivar-os/api/discovery/ingest.ts` + `packages/cultivar-os/src/pages/DiscoveryInspect.tsx`

**v0 flow (fully operational):**
1. David opens `/discovery/inspect` (internal, URL is the gate — no auth wall)
2. Enters prospect URL, vertical, optional pain point
3. Engine runs: website fetch → Haiku identity pass → Sonnet deep analysis → Sonnet synthesis email
4. David reviews profile + draft email in DiscoveryInspect
5. Enters prospect email → clicks "Send analysis" → Resend delivers the silent partner email

**Engine:**
- `discovery/adapters/website.ts` — fetches URL, extracts text (Chrome headers, fallbacks to /about)
- `discovery/engine.ts` — `runIdentity()` (Haiku, fast), `runAnalysis()` (Sonnet, deep); routes through `packages/shared/src/ai/execute.ts`
- `discovery/synthesis.ts` — `runSynthesis()`: BusinessDiscoveryProfile → SilentPartnerAnalysis; routes through `execute.ts`
- `discovery/seed.ts` — `seedServiceOfferings()`: maps suggestedOfferings → `service_offerings` rows with `is_active=false`; idempotent

**API (cultivar-os):**
- `api/discovery/ingest.ts` — POST `/api/discovery/ingest`
  - Default: fetch → identity → analysis → synthesis → `{ identity, profile, analysis, seeded }`
  - `businessId` in body → seeds service_offerings in-memory immediately after analysis (no DB lookup)
  - `action='send'` in body → delivers analysis email via notifications module (no new Vercel function)

**Admin surface:**
- `pages/DiscoveryInspect.tsx` — form (URL + vertical + pain point) → loading stages → profile display → analysis preview → Copy button + Send section (recipient email input + "Send analysis" button)

**Notification template:**
- `notifications/templates/cultivar.ts` → `silent_partner_analysis` — transactional email, TRACE default branding (not LAWNS). Pre-rendered HTML from synthesis passes through.

**⚠️ Env vars required for live email delivery (not yet set in Vercel):** `RESEND_API_KEY` + `FROM_EMAIL`. Without them, `sendNotification` runs in demo mode: logs the send, returns `{ ok: true, demo: true }` — no actual email delivery.

**remaining:**
- Discovery persistence — NOT built. `seed.ts` wired in-memory via `ingest.ts`; v0 admin sends analysis live with no retained copy. DB persistence (`discovery_sessions`, `business_discovery_profiles`), seed-at-signup via lookup, and retained session copies = v2 (gated surface + one-auth). GAP not debt. Horizon: v2/later.

### Discrepancy-Compare — entered data vs LIVE site (capability 1.1) — added 2026-06-19

**What:** During onboarding, compares the business data an owner ENTERED against what their OWN live website actually says, and surfaces honest, **hedged** discrepancies ("Your site shows X for your phone, but you entered Y — should we update it?"). Closes the gap between stale entered data and the public site without ever asserting a correction.
**Status:** ✅ BUILDER-COMPLETE (2026-06-19); owner-proof = run against a real entered-vs-site mismatch. **Type:** capability | **Vertical:** cross-vertical (shared).
**Location:** `packages/shared/src/discovery/compare.ts` (+ `compare.test.ts`, 17/17), exported from `discovery/index.ts`; AI capability `discovery_compare` in `ai/capabilities.ts`.
- `compareEnteredVsSite(entered, {apiKey})` — interrogates the **LIVE** site via `fetchWebsiteContent` (no cache/history; `fetchedAt` carried through proves freshness), extracts site values through the AI gateway, returns D-9-gated discrepancies.
- **Honesty (D-9), three gates in `filterDiscrepancies`:** (1) site must state a value; (2) entered vs site must genuinely differ (`looksSame` ignores case/punctuation/spacing); (3) confidence must clear threshold (default drops `low`). The owner-facing message is built by `buildDiscrepancyMessage` — always a QUESTION, never declares which value is correct (hedge = code guarantee, not model prose). Unreachable site → zero fabricated discrepancies, AI not even called.
- Emits `[TRACE:DISCOVERY]` (compare:start / compare:unreachable / compare:done) ON by default.

### Sandbox Seeder — branded 7-day sample data (capability 1.2, onboarding Phase 2) — added 2026-06-19

**What:** Makes a brand-new vertical's dashboard ALIVE on arrival instead of empty — seeds a believable, BRANDED 7-day slice of sample sales/activity under the business's own name. Every row is labelled sample/sandbox so it is unmistakable and EXACTLY removable; the label "comes off" when real data loads (`clear()`). Also the reusable `clear()` the Wave-2 real-populate will call.
**Status:** ✅ BUILDER-COMPLETE (2026-06-19) — `--verify` proves seed→count→clear leaves exactly zero sandbox rows and real data untouched (run against demo tenant: 6 customers / 5 plants / 5 inventory lots / 12 orders incl. 2 leakage, branded "LAWNS Tree Farm LLC"). **Type:** tool/script | **Vertical:** cultivar (tenant-agnostic by `business_id`).
**Location:** `scripts/seed-sandbox.mjs` — `seed()` / `clear()` for one `business_id`.
- Markers (clear deletes ONLY these, never real rows): `customers.source='sandbox'`, `cultivar_plants.tag_id LIKE 'SMPL-%'`, `business_inventory.sku LIKE 'SMPL-%'`, `orders.notes LIKE '[SANDBOX]%'`.
- Seeds: customers, cultivar_plants (identity), business_inventory (lots — also lights up the empty-5.1 inventory surface), orders spread over the last 7 days (varied totals, 2 leakage flags) → Dashboard today-revenue / week-orders / leakage tiles all populate.
- Emits `[TRACE:SEED]` (seed/clear with counts) ON by default.
- Usage: `node scripts/seed-sandbox.mjs --business=<uuid>` (clear+seed), `--clear`, `--verify`.

### Catalog-Populate — read live site → write their REAL catalog (capability 1.3, the onboarding wow) — added 2026-06-21

**What:** Reads a nursery's LIVE website, extracts their REAL catalog (varieties + category, per-item extraction confidence), CLEARS the sandbox sample data, and writes their actual varieties into `business_inventory` — so a new dashboard shows THEIR trees, not generic samples. The existing discovery engine extracts services/identity only; this is the missing CATALOG cut.
**Status:** ✅ BUILDER-COMPLETE (2026-06-21) — proven live against **lawnstrees.com**: 30 pages crawled → **114 real varieties** extracted (Oak, Crape Myrtle, Cypress, Redbud, Vitex …) → written to `business_inventory`, idempotent (clear→populate→clear leaves 0 orphans), real LAWNS rows untouched. **OWNER-PROOF owed** (David: run on his LAWNS tenant with the Vercel ANTHROPIC key, watch the catalog materialize on `/inventory`, then clear). **Type:** capability | **Vertical:** cross-vertical (shared), proven on cultivar.
**Location:** `packages/shared/src/discovery/catalog.ts` (crawler + AI extractor + D-9 mapping) + `discovery/populate.ts` (orchestration) + `catalog.test.ts` (35/35 adversarial), exported from `discovery/index.ts`; AI capability `discovery_catalog` (Haiku) in `ai/capabilities.ts`; runner `scripts/populate-catalog.ts`; gated table `business_discovery_profiles` (migration `20260621`, gate `scripts/verify-discovery-profiles.mjs`).
- **Crawl:** `fetchCatalogPages` does a bounded 2-level crawl (entry → hub pages like `/shop/` → category pages), reuses `fetchWebsiteContent` per page; generic link heuristics (WooCommerce `/product-category/` + hand-rolled patterns); degrades to the entry page when no catalog links exist.
- **Extract:** `extractCatalog` runs the `discovery_catalog` model per batched page → `{variety, botanical, category, confidence}`; dedups by variety (keeps highest confidence; tie-breaks prefer a SPECIFIC category over a catch-all like "All Trees", and an item carrying a botanical name).
- **Honesty (D-9 — the whole contract):** low-confidence OR no-clear-category items are **FLAGGED** (`status='review'`, reason in `notes`), never silently coerced; **price is UNKNOWN** (`unit_cost=null`, `cost_confidence='UNKNOWN'`) — never 0 (the site has no prices); **qty=0** (never fabricated); **`cultivar_plants` deliberately NOT populated** (per-specimen/QR identity doesn't exist on a bare-domain QR — writing it would be fabrication). The dashboard's `status` + `cost_confidence` badges visibly separate "sure" from "verify me".
- **Idempotent markers:** `business_inventory.sku LIKE 'DISC-%'` / `notes LIKE '[DISCOVERY]%'`. Populate clears BOTH the 1.2 sandbox (`SMPL-`/`[SANDBOX]`, reusing `clearSandbox`) and prior discovery rows before writing.
- **`business_discovery_profiles`** (gated migration) stores the full `raw_extract` (items + confidences + counts) as the honesty/audit trail — upsert on `(business_id, source_url)`.
- Emits `[TRACE:POPULATE]` (crawl / extract / clear / populate) ON by default.
- **Size-variant parser real-page PROOF (2026-06-30, verification artifact — NOT a capability change):** `extractSizeVariants` (the deterministic, no-AI WooCommerce size parser, ledger #62) is now proven against the **real** LAWNS Vitex page, not just synthetic fixtures. Pinned offline fixture `packages/shared/src/discovery/__fixtures__/lawns-vitex-real.html` (real `https://lawnstrees.com/product/shoal-creek-vitex/`, fetched 2026-06-30, **sanitized**: Mailchimp API key + connected-site ids + GA/GTM ids + business phone redacted — size markup `data-product_variations`/`<select>` preserved byte-for-byte) + proof script `scripts/prove-vitex-variants.ts` (default OFFLINE/deterministic; `--live` flag for a network re-fetch). Asserts the **JSON-primary** path yields exactly `["5 Gallon","15 Gallon","30 Gallon","45 Gallon"]` in order, no invented sizes / no enum coercion, and the dry-run rows populate WOULD write (one per Vitex × size, `variant_group=parent slug`, honesty contract qty 0 / cost UNKNOWN) — **10/10**, DRY-RUN only (nothing written to any catalog). Does NOT change capability state: live populate-into-real-catalog + the count-side picker owner-prove on populated data remain OWED/gated.
- **Dup-size data-quality surfacing at WRITE time (2026-06-30, ledger #74, BUILDER-COMPLETE, app-code-only):** populate now DETECTS a `(variant_group, size)` collision before insert and SURFACES it, closing the silent dead-end the stress battery (#73 recon) found — two rows sharing one `variant_group` + the same `size` made a variety silently uncountable-by-scan at count time (`detectSizeCollision`'s all-distinct check fails → UNKNOWN, indistinguishable from never-seen). **Refuse-to-guess is UNCHANGED** (`detectSizeCollision` untouched; we detect + surface, never dedupe/auto-pick — both rows still written). New pure exported `findDuplicateSizeGroups(rows)` in `populate.ts` runs after row-expansion → emits `[TRACE:POPULATE] data-quality:dup-size` + adds `PopulateResult.dataQuality.dupSizeGroups` + `raw_extract.counts.dupSizeGroups` (audit trail) + the `api/discovery/ingest.ts` `action=populate` response, so the owner sees it in the populate report before it reaches a count. Why populate is the right place: it's the importer, and `extractSizeVariants` already dedupes within one product, so the realistic dup is two varieties bound to one `sourceSlug`. `populate.test.ts` **9/9** (clean distinct sizes → no finding; same group+same size → flagged count 2; two varieties one slug → flagged; null/parent rows never flagged; case-insensitive size match; cross-group same size NOT flagged). `npm run verify` exit 0 zero NET-NEW. Deferred (David's call): **B** count-time UNKNOWN distinction in `InventoryCount.tsx`; **C** DB unique constraint on `(business_id, variant_group, size)` (migration). No schema, no migration.

### CSV Catalog Import — a grower's price list → `business_inventory` (the second half of onboarding, capability 1.3) — added 2026-07-23

**What:** The MANUAL counterpart to Catalog-Populate: instead of scraping a live site, the owner uploads a grower's CSV price list, maps its columns to the catalog spine, reviews a per-row PLAN, and Accepts. Inventory-only, owner-only, desktop, one file at a time, CSV only. The other, deliberate half — CUSTOMER import — stays a GAP.
**Status:** ✅ BUILDER-COMPLETE (2026-07-23) — `npm run verify` exit 0 zero net-new; **80/0 new tests + 128/0 regression**; `build:cultivar` exit 0. **DEFECT FIX #150 (2026-07-23):** the matcher no longer sources on the foreign item # (name+size decide; item # corroborates/conflicts only, kept as a note not our sku — D-47) and SIZE is normalized before comparison via the shared `utils/sizeLabel` (COMPARISON-ONLY, D-23; closes tech-debt #56). **OWNER-PROOF owed (21 cards — 14 for #148 + 5 for #149 + cards 20/21 for #150)** → `docs/owner-tests/inventory-full-surface-test.md` → `## SURFACE: csv-import`, provable with the fixture WITHOUT a console. **Type:** capability | **Vertical:** cross-vertical (shared core), surface on cultivar.
**Location:** PURE core `packages/shared/src/import/` — `parseCsv.ts` (hand-rolled RFC-4180: BOM/CRLF/quoted-comma/embedded-newline/blank-line, 18/0), `columnMap.ts` (the 4-rung ladder + ONE platform-wide synonym dictionary + the 🔴 load-bearing-money flag, 20/0), `importPlan.ts` (per-row verdicts, 34/0), `index.ts`. Surface `packages/cultivar-os/src/pages/InventoryImport.tsx` (two-step wizard) + `pages/importWrites.ts` (the write orchestration). Migration `supabase/migrations/20260723_inventory_import_columns.sql` (GATED — David applies).
- **The two new columns (D-24 + D-9, both settled, not re-derived):** `price_basis text` is the missing pair-half of D-35's `sell_price` (a bare price is a number with no unit); `attributes jsonb` is D-24's flexible-edge BAG, finally built — a grower's descriptive columns (Sun, Height, Zone, notes) the system never operates on, keyed by their own header, value verbatim. Both nullable, no CHECK, no new policy (RLS inherits — the 20260628/20260707 precedent).
- **The 4-rung mapping ladder:** L1 exact → L2 synonym (the ONE configurable thing, platform-wide, never per-tenant) → L3 shape (inferred from VALUES; PROPOSES, never auto-applies) → L4 unmapped (keep-as-attribute default, or ignore). Every mapping carries WHICH rung produced it; the owner overrides any of them. 🔴 A blob field may never be money — a currency-shaped unmapped column (Wholesale/Cost/Net/Landed) is flagged "looks like a real field; nothing computes on it," held in the bag (making it a real column is a spine decision + a migration).
- **Per-row verdicts (D-49 reused, not re-derived):** FILL a stub · UPDATE a lot · CREATE a sibling + auto-group the parent · AMBIGUOUS (owner picks/skips) · CONFLICT (a physically-counted lot — HELD, never overwritten without an explicit per-row action; **David's ruling 2026-07-23**) · REFUSED (no name / size-required). `$0.00` and blank prices → UNKNOWN, never 0 (D-9); a blank qty never zeroes a lot.
- **STD-011 — the matcher is NOT forked:** the name/size ladder is one pure `resolveAgainstCatalog` extracted from `resolveStockLine` (which now delegates to it; `resolveAgainstCatalog.test.ts` 8/0 guards it), and the FILL/UPDATE/CREATE branch is `resolveCountTarget` (D-49) reused.
- **Writes ride EXISTING RPCs only — ZERO new api-fn (12/12), zero ledger forks:** CREATE = `count_promote_create_inventory`(qty 0 → `opening_balance` genesis) then `adjust_inventory_manual`(kind='import') to move stock in — so an import never masquerades as a physical `count_reconcile` (which is the CONFLICT signal); qty via `adjust_inventory_manual`(kind='import', reason names the file); attributes/size via the shared `persistInventoryPatch`; **price/basis via the gated `import_write_price` RPC** (#149). Partial failure is surfaced per row (#69). `[TRACE:IMPORT]` ON by default.
- **BULK PRICE GATE — `import_pricing` (#149, David's ruling 2026-07-23):** bulk price import defaults OWNER-only, grantable to a manager on /team. 🔴 **A blast-radius control, NOT a price wall** — `business_inventory` is already `view_costs`-gated, so a manager can already edit `sell_price` one cell at a time; this gates the BULK write only. WIRED server-side: `import_write_price(lot,business,actor,sell_price,price_basis)` (SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path=''`) checks `has_permission_for(business, PASSED-actor, 'import_pricing')` (the passed-actor, owner-inclusive analog of `has_permission` — the D-50 scar honored). A soft `applied:false` is the server refusal; the manager still imports quantities/attributes; the client "won't be saved" notice is a courtesy, not the authority. The existing member policy is UNTOUCHED (additive RPC only). Migration `20260723_inventory_import_pricing_gate.sql` **GATED — apply AFTER `20260723_inventory_import_columns.sql`**.
- **Own door (2026-06-21 record §2.6):** `/inventory/import` is reached from the inventory grid's "Import CSV" button (**VIEW_COSTS-gated per #149, not owner-only** — a manager imports quantities); it does NOT touch ReceiptKeeper and is not reachable from "Snap a receipt."
- **Fixture:** `docs/owner-tests/fixtures/test-grower-pricelist-FIXTURE.csv` (+ FABRICATED-data README) exercises every card's case.

### Cost-Discovery — cost-to-produce reasoning (Opus) → `business_inventory` cost + confidence — added 2026-06-21

**What:** A discovery capability that drives a short question flow REASONING about a grower's cost-to-produce for ONE inventory line (e.g. the LAWNS 30-gal Live Oak) and writes `business_inventory.unit_cost` + `cost_confidence`. Each owner answer re-runs the reasoning, so confidence SHARPENS as inputs accumulate — typically ESTIMATED (a stated guess) → DERIVED (computed from confirmed inputs). Routed to **Opus** (reasoning-under-uncertainty) via the existing per-capability model routing, with the per-business `business_modules.config` override still honored. Mirrors the catalog-discovery honesty posture, pointed at COST instead of variety. NOT a schema change — `unit_cost` (nullable) + `cost_confidence` (CONFIRMED|DERIVED|ESTIMATED|UNKNOWN) already exist (`20260612_business_assets_inventory_cost_confidence.sql`).
**Status:** ✅ BUILDER-COMPLETE (2026-06-21) — `costDiscovery.test.ts` **26/26** adversarial (no live AI) + `scripts/verify-cost-discovery.ts` **9/9** against LIVE `business_inventory` on the service key (ESTIMATED written → SHARPENS to DERIVED → forced-CONFIRMED **BLOCKED**, row unchanged → UNKNOWN leaves the line as-is → cleanup). **OWNER-PROOF owed** (David: run a cost-discovery pass through real UI under real RLS on a real line, watch confidence move ESTIMATED→DERIVED as he answers, and see it refuse to claim CONFIRMED without a receipt — needs a small UI panel on `/inventory` as the surface). **Type:** capability | **Vertical:** cross-vertical (shared), proven on cultivar.
**Location:** `packages/shared/src/discovery/costDiscovery.ts` (engine: prompt + code honesty-guard + turn driver + writer) + `costDiscovery.test.ts` (26/26), exported from `discovery/index.ts`; AI capability `discovery_cost` (**Opus `claude-opus-4-8`**) in `ai/capabilities.ts`; wired into the existing `api/discovery/ingest.ts` as `action='cost-discovery'` (one turn) + `action='cost-apply'` (write) — **no new Vercel function** (12-fn ceiling, tech-debt #41); proof `scripts/verify-cost-discovery.ts`.
- **Honesty contract (enforced in CODE, not just the prompt — the real risk surface):**
  - **NEVER CONFIRMED:** `guardReasoning` RAISES `CostConfidenceViolation` on any model attempt to claim CONFIRMED (reserved for receipt-linked cost, D-9 count-once seam); the writer re-rejects it at the DB boundary. Proven: a forced-CONFIRMED model output is blocked and the row is left untouched.
  - **NEVER fabricate:** a missing / non-numeric / zero / negative cost collapses to UNKNOWN (null) — unknown stays UNKNOWN, never written as $0.
  - **ESTIMATED vs DERIVED:** ESTIMATED = owner's stated guess; DERIVED = computed from confirmed inputs and carries the `derivation` arithmetic. A DERIVED claim with no shown arithmetic is DEMOTED to ESTIMATED — a guess is never dressed up as a derivation.
  - **Declined leaves it where it was:** `applyCostReasoning` makes NO write on an UNKNOWN turn — the line is left exactly as it was, honestly labeled (never clobbered, never fabricated).
- **Tenant-safe write:** updates `business_inventory` scoped by `(id, business_id)` — never cross-tenant.
- Emits `[TRACE:COSTDISC]` (turn / guard / apply) ON by default per the standing owner instruction.

**What:** Sign-off artifact for the role-permission hardening build that follows — NOT a build. Decides the **four permissions** (view_wages, view_pricing_config, view_costs, view_margin — view_margin requires view_costs), **role defaults** (Owner=all four; Manager=costs+margin not pricing-config/wages; Tech=none), and the **hybrid enforcement by data class** (HARD data-layer wall via RLS + column masking for wages + pricing_config; ROLE-AWARE RESPONSE SHAPING for costs + margin because the server consumes unit_cost in checkout #11/#12 + dashboard rollups and a flat RLS-deny would NULL totals across 14 multi-role SELECT sites; render gates are the top convenience layer only). Binding doctrine: DEFAULT-DENY · SINGLE CHOKEPOINT (can()/checkPermission()) · RESTRICTIVE-FIRST/EXPAND-ONLY · PROOF BAR (low-role direct query refused at network response, not render-absence).

Decision LOCKED; **enforcement unbuilt** (build owes: mint+seed four permissions + backfill current owners before flipping on · wire dormant chokepoint · column-masking approach chosen at build · per-site hand-verify the 14 SELECT sites · resolve checkout public-path unit_cost shaping). ### Standard — STD-011 ONE CANONICAL REPRESENTATION PER FACT (DOC ONLY) — added 2026-06-22

**What:** New numbered standard in STANDARDS.md — NOT a build. A single-meaning fact (access/state booleans, tenant predicates, enum/status value sets, driftable config values) MUST have exactly ONE canonical representation referenced everywhere, never re-derived or duplicated; two representations of one fact can silently disagree, and in access/boundary/value facts that silence IS the leak. Encodes: state→one column via one shared helper (e.g. SECURITY DEFINER `is_active_member()`, which also resolves RLS recursion); enum→one allowed-value definition; config→one home per the config doctrine. Enforcement: a new sensitive column/policy re-deriving a fact = INCOMPLETE; a consistency sweep is a recognized correctness-hardening tool. Promotes AC-4 to a data-integrity invariant with a security consequence.
**Status:** ✅ Standard written into STANDARDS.md (§STD-011 + ENFORCEMENT row + CHANGELOG v1.9). **Type:** standard | **Vertical:** cross-vertical.
**Origin:** the membership-active RLS consistency sweep — `data/grower-scan/member-rls-consistency-audit.md` (3 spellings of "active member"; `md_self` omitted the active filter → revoked member kept self-device access). **Recorded open item:** owner-only operational tables (orders/customers/plants/order_items + pre-2026-06 tables) have NO member policy → Staff RLS-blocked despite `view_orders`/`qr_checkout`; next hardening item is a PRODUCT decision (what does Staff see, scoped how, PII?). Cross-pointer in tech-debt only.
**Cross-refs:** [STANDARDS.md](../STANDARDS.md) §STD-011 · AC-4 / AC-2 / AC-3 · config doctrine.

### Recon — the SEVEN write paths to `businesses`, the platform's RLS anchor (DOC ONLY) — added 2026-08-21

**📏 RECON DOC, NOT A CAPABILITY. Nothing shipped** — no code, no schema, no migration, no policy, no cap, no fix, **no declaration** (declaring is a decision). Zero diff under `packages/`, `api/`, `supabase/`. → `docs/audits/businesses-write-paths-recon-2026-08-21.md`, ledger **#189**, measured at `8e0224f`.

**WHY IT OUTRANKED THE REST:** measurement #188 reported `businesses` at **7 undeclared write paths** — not among `verify-write-paths`' declared failures, so **not tracked debt**. `businesses` is the RLS anchor: `business_id` is what AC-2 scopes to and AC-3 makes absolute, and the row also holds `address` (the delivery origin) and `business_type` (which drives `verticalsForBusinessType` — **which tiles a tenant sees**).

🔴 **THE COLUMN MATRIX IS THE DECIDING ARTEFACT AND IT SPLITS CLEAN — THREE DISJOINT ACTS, NOT SEVEN COPIES OF ONE:** **CREATION** (`id`/`owner_id`/`business_type`/`trial_started_at`) reachable from **two** sites and no others · **IDENTITY** (`name`/`address`/`phone`/`email`/`website`) from **five** · **ACCOUNTING** (`accounting_*`) from **four**, with **zero overlap in either direction**. The genuine duplication is **exactly two sites** — `OnboardingWizard.tsx:608` and `DiscoveryGlimpse.tsx:183` — **both strict SUBSETS of the narrow RPC that already exists**, and both direct table UPDATEs of the kind that RPC was built to replace. **Per the #168 precedent the honest floor looks like THREE, not one — and NO MERGE ORDER IS PROPOSED, because that is David's ruling and the matrix is its input.**

🟢 **THE SCREENSHOT QUESTION IS ANSWERED AND THE ANSWER IS NO: the section filter did NOT fragment the writer.** `/settings/all|business|accounting|services` are **ONE route** (`router.tsx:176`) and **ONE component** with a `section` prop filtering which card renders; the profile Save calls one function calling one RPC. Accounting's columns come from the OAuth callback (a machine act); Services writes a different table. **And `set_business_profile` is the model working** — gated `settings:update`, **audits its denials**, column list IS the security boundary, with the migration saying so at the line: *owner_id / accounting_* / business_type UNREACHABLE from here BY CONSTRUCTION*.

🔴 **TWO LIVE DEFECTS, NAMED AND NOT FIXED — the same dropped column twice.** `20260727e:45` dropped `businesses.tax_rate`. **D1: `scripts/test-member-login.mjs:144` still INSERTS it** → `42703` at **step 5 of 34**, so the **29 steps after it are unreachable**; it survived because **the harness is not in `npm run verify`**. **D2: `BusinessProvider.tsx:124` still TYPES `tax_rate: number` non-nullable** — benign only because the provider uses `select('*')` and no select names the column. ⚠️ **Both hinge on 27e's apply state, read from the ⚡ front-page and NOT catalog-verified — there is no `psql` here, and one query settles it.**

**Other answers worth the index:** **Q6** — six of nine app sites don't check affected rows and are already baselined **unchecked**; LATENT because `businesses` has no member UPDATE policy and every member-reachable path routes through the RPC, **live the day a manager reaches a direct update**. **Q8** — `owner_id` is safe but **implicitly** (`businesses_owner_update` is `USING` with no `WITH CHECK`; Postgres applies USING as the UPDATE check), while 🔴 **`business_type` is protected by nothing but the absence of code that writes it**. **Q9** — exactly **one** function writes the table, **no triggers**, and **7 is a FLOOR not a total** (§6 r17's class; the schema-snapshot checker is OWED). **Q10** — **seven** parallel write/type enumerations plus **twenty** select sites carrying **fourteen** distinct column lists, none derived (more than `customers`' six before E6 phase A). **Q11, from git not inference** — `businesses` was **never declared**; the count moved 6→7 when `13d920d` folded RPC callers into their targets. **Q5** — the #168 compare-against-the-draft defect is **ABSENT**: no site performs an unchanged-check at all.

**Type:** recon (DOC ONLY) | **Vertical:** shared. **Cross-refs:** ledger #189 · #188 · #168 · A2 · AC-2/AC-3 · §6 r17.

### Financial Permission Wall — data-layer enforcement (RLS primitives + child-table splits) — updated 2026-06-22

**What:** The data-layer enforcement of the role-financial-permissions decision (above). Two SECURITY DEFINER primitives + per-table RLS so a low-role member's cost/wage/pricing read is refused at the DATA layer (network response), not just hidden at render.
- **Primitives (canonical, owned by postgres, `search_path=''`, EXECUTE→authenticated):** `is_active_member(business_id)` (membership truth — `20260622_is_active_member_canonical_rls.sql`) + `has_permission(business_id, perm)` (active membership AND `permissions ? perm`, jsonb array-of-strings — `20260622_oauth_secrets_relocation_and_cost_wall.sql`). Policies compose them: `is_active_member(X) AND has_permission(X,'view_costs')` (membership-then-permission, composed not fused).
- **HARD-WALL child-table splits (`20260621_financial_wall_phase2.sql`):** `labor_resource_wages` (HR pay, gated `view_wages`) + `business_pricing_config` (pricing recipe, gated `view_pricing_config`) — sensitive values moved off the member-readable parent into role-gated child rows; member policies decomposed onto the two primitives 2026-06-22.
- **view_costs table gates (2026-06-22):** 6 cost tables (`cost_objects`, `business_inventory`, `cost_object_edges`, `cost_object_assignments`, `business_service_log`, `receipts`) + `labor_resources` (view_wages) — member policy = `is_active_member AND has_permission`. (The view_costs data gate was NEVER built before this — cost tables had been membership-only; recon `data/grower-scan/cost-wall-leak-scope.md`.)
- **Render layer (top convenience tier only, NOT the boundary):** `PermissionRoute permission={VIEW_COSTS}` wraps `/costs /inventory /assets /operating-costs /pmi` (`packages/cultivar-os/src/router.tsx`); chokepoint `can()`/`has_permission()` in `BusinessProvider`. Permission constants: `packages/shared/src/auth/financialPermissions.ts`.

**Status:** ✅ READ-WALL OWNER-PROVEN (Staff HAR: cost reads return 200 [], RLS row-filter; tamper 3/0; migrations live). ✅ WRITE-WALL — data-layer ENFORCED + the one bypass CLOSED (Gate-3b, 2026-06-22): the cost/wage member policies are `FOR ALL` with `has_permission` in `USING`+`WITH CHECK` (`20260622_…cost_wall.sql:141-212`) → anon-key INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE refused for a member lacking the permission (recon-confirmed; write-tamper proof `scripts/verify-write-wall.ts`). The lone service-key bypass (costDiscovery `cost-apply`) is now caller-permission-gated (`api/discovery/ingest.ts` requires `view_costs` resolved from the auth context). Residual: render-layer button gates (operating-cost "+cost" shown to staff though the write is RLS-refused) = defense-in-depth, tech-debt #46.2. Enforces the D-009 moat on reads AND writes. Staff HAR detail: `trace_staff_only`, `isOwner:false`, `effectivePermissions` Array(3), no `view_costs`; every cost read (`cost_objects`, `business_inventory…unit_cost`, `business_pricing_config`) returned `200 []`. Artifact: `cultivar-os_vercel_app.har`. **Type:** capability (RLS/security) | **Vertical:** shared.

**Phase 3 Part A (render layer)** — ⏳ BUILDER-COMPLETE, OWNER-PROVEN OWED-AFTER-DEPLOY. Code committed (`8918fe8`): `router.tsx:82` wraps `/costs|/inventory|/assets|/operating-costs|/pmi` in `<PermissionRoute permission={VIEW_COSTS}>`; `Dashboard.tsx:145` shapes the query (`.select(canViewCosts ? 'qty, unit_cost' : 'qty')`). In local `dist`; LIVE Vercel deploy + fresh Staff HAR owed: expect cost requests absent from the dashboard, the dashboard inventory query qty-only, and direct nav to gated routes redirects. (Part C grower-import — checkout cost-as-price is the 2nd symptom of the `sell_price`/`price_basis` gap → `docs/decisions/2026-06-21-grower-import-and-mobile-roles.md` + `data/grower-scan/inventory-fit-map.md`.)
**Cross-refs:** recon `data/grower-scan/cost-wall-leak-scope.md` · decision `docs/decisions/2026-06-21-role-financial-permissions.md` · STD-011.

### `SECURITY DEFINER` Function Layer — swept CLEAN, and NOT by inheritance — recorded 2026-07-28

**What:** The function-execution layer, catalog-verified across **all 28 functions in `public`** (David's read, relayed and attributed per STD-021 v2.8 / the #159b relay seam — not independently re-derived). **Zero code, zero migration, zero schema — a FINDING.** Ledger **#161**. It is written down because an unrecorded clean sweep is indistinguishable from a check nobody ran.
- **23 are `SECURITY DEFINER`, and EVERY ONE carries `search_path=''`.** The schema-hijack path — a definer function resolving an unqualified name against an attacker-controlled `search_path` — is closed **across the board**, not case by case.
- **EVERY WRITE FUNCTION CARRIES AN EXPLICIT ACL AND NOT ONE GRANTS `anon`.** Ordinary write set: `postgres, authenticated, service_role`. **Five are tighter still — `postgres, service_role` only: `adjust_inventory_qty`, `emit_inventory_movement`, `discovery_create_inventory`, `discovery_rescan_clear`, `record_order_event`** — the D-50 ledger primitives. Exactly the right five to be service-only; a deliberate narrowing, not an accident.
- **`anon=X` appears in precisely two places, both NECESSARY.** (1) The four PREDICATES — `has_permission`, `has_permission_for`, `is_active_member`, `is_member_of` — because **RLS `USING` clauses must evaluate them for anon sessions on the public QR path**; revoking anon's EXECUTE would break the customer-facing scan, not harden it. (2) Trigger functions, which are **not PostgREST-callable** at all. ⚠️ **Recorded so nobody later "hardens" these by reading `anon=X` as a defect.**
- 🔴 **THIS WAS NOT INHERITED SAFETY — the most important line here.** The default privilege on `public` grants **EXECUTE to `anon` for every new function**. Left alone the sweep would have read the opposite way. The clean state is the residue of someone applying `REVOKE ALL FROM public` / `GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated` **consistently, including on functions predating this week.**
- ⚠️ **IT RE-READS #157.** Five RLS-bypass paths were swept; this is the fifth and the only one clean on first look. The other four had findings (the eight service-key handlers · the auto-updatable view · the alias-layer escalation · the table-privilege grants). **The SQL layer — policies, definer functions, function ACLs — was built carefully throughout**, so the API-layer gap is an **ANOMALY, not a pattern**: the week's defects clustered where the app reaches *around* the database (service-key handlers taking the tenant from the request), not in the database's own defences.

### Client-Role Privilege Baseline — `TRUNCATE`/`REFERENCES` revoked from anon + authenticated — added 2026-07-28

**What:** The table-privilege layer *beneath* RLS, for the two PostgREST client roles. Migration `20260728_revoke_truncate_references_public.sql` — §1 executable (applied by David by hand; this file is the replayable repo record), §2 (`ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES`) **deliberately PENDING** his `pg_default_acl` read. Ledger **#160**. Zero app code, zero schema change, ZERO api-fn (12/12).
- **WHY IT IS A SEPARATE LAYER AT ALL:** **RLS governs SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and nothing else.** `TRUNCATE` is outside it — no policy can filter one, and a `FOR EACH ROW` trigger does not fire on one. So a TRUNCATE grant is a privilege the security model is structurally unable to see. `anon` held it on ~45 tables. `REFERENCES` (create a FK *against* a table) is likewise not something any client needs — FKs are authored in migrations, as postgres.
- **LATENT, NOT LIVE — stated so this is read as neither an incident nor busywork.** PostgREST exposes no TRUNCATE verb, so no request carrying the anon key could invoke it. The exposure was the standing grant against a future path (a direct connection, a new client library, a careless `SECURITY DEFINER` function).
- **THE THIRD PAYMENT OF ONE LESSON.** `audit_log` (`20260624`) and `business_inventory_ledger` (`20260720:207-209`) each revoked TRUNCATE *one table at a time* on discovering it. This generalises those two scars across the schema.
- **WHAT THE V4 SWEEP FOUND** (David's live catalog read, relayed — attributed as such in the migration header per STD-021 v2.8 / the #159b relay seam, not claimed as an independent read): **every base table has RLS enabled** — no table was naked — and **the grant pattern is Supabase's uniform project-creation default, not a per-table choice**, which is why the fix is one statement rather than forty-five. **The view (#159) was the only object in the schema where a grant was the SOLE line of defence** (a view has no policies, so its grants *were* its security — hence a DROP there and a revoke here).
- **THE GOOD PATTERN ALREADY EXISTS, IN EXACTLY TWO PLACES:** `audit_log` and `business_inventory_ledger` carried INSERT/REFERENCES/SELECT/TRIGGER only — write privileges deliberately revoked — so append-only is enforced at the **privilege layer AND** by the trigger. **Two independent layers; the only place in the schema where that happens.** ⚠️ Intended, not a slip: the blanket revoke narrows those two further, to INSERT/SELECT/TRIGGER.
- **BLAST RADIUS (corpus + method, STD-021):** case-insensitive `TRUNCATE` grep across `supabase/migrations/`, `scripts/`, `packages/shared/src`, `packages/cultivar-os/src`, `packages/cultivar-os/api`, `api` → **one executing consumer**, `scripts/wipe-for-person-spine.sql`, which runs **as postgres** and is unaffected. Every other hit is prose.
- ✅ **§2 RESOLVED (`20260728b`, applied 2026-07-28) — THE DEFAULT ACL WAS REAL, so §1 alone would have decayed at the next `CREATE TABLE`.** `pg_default_acl` named **`postgres`** as the granting role; `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA public REVOKE TRUNCATE, REFERENCES ON TABLES FROM anon, authenticated` applied. **Verified by the ACL LETTERS, not by the statement succeeding:** the `postgres`/`'r'` row now reads **`anon=arwdtm` — `D` (TRUNCATE) and `x` (REFERENCES) gone.** `FOR ROLE` was load-bearing exactly as predicted — the bare form would have silently no-opped had the entry belonged elsewhere. **NEW FILE, NOT AN EDIT (§6 r1):** `20260728`'s §2 stated that if §1 landed first it becomes an appended migration; it did. §1's revoke is repeated inside `20260728b` deliberately — **a default ACL governs only FUTURE objects and never retroactively fixes one already created**, so on a rebuild the pair must land together.
- ✅ **CATALOG-VERIFIED 2026-07-28 (David, live at postgres) — V1–V4 ALL CLEAN, the privilege layer is CLOSED.** **V1** ZERO rows: no TRUNCATE/REFERENCES for either client role anywhere in `public`. **V2** ordinary CRUD untouched — `anon` 45 tables / `authenticated` 46, both DELETE/INSERT/SELECT/TRIGGER/UPDATE; RLS still doing exactly the filtering it always did. ⚠️ *The 45-vs-46 gap is explained, not a defect:* `business_inventory_ledger_events` (anon revoked by hand at #159, authenticated still on it) — **resolves when `20260727f` drops the view.** **V3** `audit_log` + `business_inventory_ledger` at INSERT/SELECT/TRIGGER per role — the tightest set in the schema, and it got tighter. **V4 🔴 THE DURABILITY PROOF PASSES:** a fresh table inherited exactly **five** privileges and **no TRUNCATE, no REFERENCES** — the default ACL is **LIVE *and* narrowed**, which is the only outcome meaning "fixed" (a silently no-opped statement would have given seven; an absent default, none).
- 🔴 **THE RESIDUAL IS UNFIXABLE BY STATEMENT AND BECOMES A WORKFLOW RULE. The same ALTER against `supabase_admin` was DENIED** — Supabase owns that role and grants no membership in it. **`supabase_admin` is the role that creates tables when SUPABASE does: the dashboard TABLE EDITOR.** Its default ACL still grants TRUNCATE and REFERENCES to `anon`, so **a table created there arrives carrying both again — silently, looking exactly like every other table.** ⇒ ***CREATE TABLES THROUGH MIGRATIONS, NOT THE DASHBOARD TABLE EDITOR.*** Not style: the migration path runs as `postgres` (default ACL now correct), the table-editor path as `supabase_admin` (cannot be corrected). Same table, different privileges, decided by which window it was typed into.
- ✏️ **SCOPE CORRECTED 2026-07-28 BY THE V4 PROBE — and the probe is what narrowed it.** The probe was created through the **SQL editor** and inherited the **postgres** default, so **the SQL editor is NOT the gap**; two surfaces are colloquially "the dashboard" and only the table editor re-grants. The first phrasing would have banned a safe path **and taught a rule the next probe visibly contradicts — which is how a correct rule gets discarded wholesale.** Corrected in the `20260728b` header (comment-only, statements untouched, dated inline — the file was already applied) and in **CLAUDE.md §6 r17**.
- ⚠️ **AN EXTERNAL CONTROL EXISTS, AND IT IS NOT OURS:** Supabase's SQL editor warns before a `CREATE TABLE` without RLS — *"Clients using anon or authenticated keys may be able to access public.&lt;table&gt;."* The platform guards the same shape we spent the week finding. Recorded as existing, **not as something we built and not as something to rely on**: dismissible, present on one surface only, and about RLS rather than the privilege layer this entry covers.
- ⚠️ **THE DETECTOR IS WRITTEN; ITS AUTOMATED HOME IS OWED — recorded as owed, not as done.** A `public` table carrying TRUNCATE/REFERENCES for `anon` after this migration was created outside the migration path (its owner will read `supabase_admin` — **the privilege is the fingerprint**). The query is in `20260728b` in final form, but the **#159b schema-snapshot checker does not exist**, and **`scripts/verify-universals.mjs` structurally cannot host it: it reads migration `.sql` from the repo, and this is a live-catalog claim** (STD-021 — privileges are catalog facts the repo cannot answer). **⚠️ It must read `pg_class.relacl` via `aclexplode`, NEVER `information_schema.role_table_grants`** — that view only exposes grants where the querying role is grantor or a member of the grantee, so from a non-member role it **returns zero rows on a database full of violations**: a false green manufactured by the checker's own permissions.
- ✅ **STD-022 probe (Assertion C) — RAN 2026-07-28 AND PASSED.** A throwaway `CREATE TABLE` inside `BEGIN…ROLLBACK`: the only thing that distinguishes *"the default ACL was fixed"* from *"there was never one to fix."* Five privileges arrived, two did not. It also produced the scope correction above, which is the second thing a break-it probe buys you — it told us something we had not asked it.
- ⚠️ **SCOPE ASYMMETRY, RECORDED NOT WIDENED:** both precedents named three roles (`…, service_role`); this names two, so **`service_role` retains TRUNCATE** elsewhere. David's statement as given; broadening it is a separate decision (the sale path runs under that key).
- ⚠️ **NOT CLOSED HERE — its own build:** `anon` holds INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on ~45 tables. Probably wrong (the QR path reads a catalog; every write goes through service-key `submit.ts`) — but RLS *is* filtering those three verbs, which is the difference from TRUNCATE. Scoping it down needs a corpus check of what anon actually writes.

### Owner-Role Authority — Stage 1, ACCESS: the role reaches what the role holds (R-22) — added 2026-08-28

**What:** An OWNER-ROLE member who is **not** `businesses.owner_id` held the full computed permission set on the client and was refused by the database on three surfaces. Migration `20260828_owner_role_carries_authority.sql` (**WRITTEN, NOT APPLIED — David applies as postgres, then runs V1–V9**) closes all three, plus the invite funnel behind them.
- **THE RULING (David, 2026-08-28 — R-22):** *"Lauren needs all perms and authority to act … changing a role from manager to owner is a decision the owner makes, then the new 'owner' can administer the system and assign all roles … the perms need allow the role to assume the perms within that role."* **`owner_id` stops being an authority mechanism and becomes the account holder of last resort.** This is the 2026-07-30 ruling followed one step further: that one made the owner pass the check like everyone else, this one lets the check reach what the role is supposed to hold. 20260730c's own V6 comment predicted it — *"Retiring the policy-level owner branch is a SEPARATE build, and this is its first input."*
- **🔴 THE FINDING ABOVE THE BUILD — A MANIFEST FLIP ALONE SHIPS NOTHING.** `has_permission()` reads the **STORED** `business_members.permissions` jsonb (`20260726_permission_alias_layer.sql:264-290`); the client **COMPUTES** `OWNER_LOCKED_SET` from the manifest at page load (`BusinessProvider.tsx:725`). Flipping a status to `enforced` grows the client set, leaves the server at 54, and reproduces the *"Tax: not identified"* inversion **in the opposite direction — the client offering controls the database refuses.** So §5/§6 re-materialise every OWNER-role member **through the funnel** (`save_role_permissions('reset')`), never a direct UPDATE: the authority trigger permits `auth.uid() IS NULL`, so a hand-written UPDATE in the SQL editor **would have worked and produced no audit row.**
- **FIVE POLICIES, ONE SHAPE:** `is_active_member(business_id) AND has_permission(business_id, '<string>')`, copied from `service_offerings_member`. **NOT** the `cultivar_plants` fused owner-OR-member form, which admits every active member including STAFF; **NOT** keyed on `role = 'OWNER'`, which would bake the grant decision into each table and break on a rename. `bm_self_select` **stays** — every session resolves its own role from its own row, so removing it would black out the app below OWNER.
- **THE INVITE IS AN RPC, NOT A POLICY, AND THE REASON IS MECHANICAL.** The authority-immutability trigger is **BEFORE UPDATE ONLY** (`20260723:169`), so **INSERT is not covered** — a member INSERT policy on `business_members` would have been a permission-granting side door with no funnel and no audit row, taking its `permissions` array **from the browser**. `create_invitation` (SECURITY DEFINER) authorises on `team:create`, **resolves the array server-side from the role floor**, writes both rows in one transaction, and audits. 🔴 **This closes a hole that existed BEFORE the pass**, not one it created: the array already travelled from the client.
- **🔴 THE SERVICES HARD DELETE IS REMOVED, NOT GATED (R2 stands, David confirming).** `order_service_selections.service_offering_id` is `NOT NULL REFERENCES` with **no `ON DELETE` clause** (`20260529_businesses_f:69`), so it raised **23503 on any offering ever sold** and **permanently destroyed** one that had not — no tombstone, no ledger, no audit, nowhere near the bar `inventory:delete` cleared. Retire-by-flag already existed as `toggleOffering`, so the ✕ went rather than becoming a second control doing one thing (§6 r8). **Stated cost:** a mistyped service can no longer be removed; it goes Off and stays.
- **THREE ROSTER WRITES LOCKED WITH AN EXPLANATION.** Remove · Deactivate · Set-phone are direct writes still fenced on `bm_owner_all`. Widening the roster READ alone would have shipped **three visible failures** in the same commit that fixed the read (§1.6 item 5, newly visible since `393682a`). `rosterAuthority.ts` is the ONE decision; both team surfaces consult it. **David's note for the next pass:** Remove and Deactivate are **access control, not data edits** — they belong in the funnel; Set-phone is benign.
- **🔴 TWO CAPS REFUSED THE BUILD AND BOTH WERE RIGHT.** **capQ**: three strings left the applied `20260727` R-B2 snapshot — **tech-debt #90's exact blocker, hit from the other side**. Resolved by a **self-pruning declaration** (`r-b2-wired-since-declarations.json`): a declaration for a string still marked declared-unwired, or one the snapshot never held, **fails the build**. Not by editing an applied migration (§6 r1) and not by deleting the assertion. **capA**: `parseOwnerBundle` scanned the array body with a bare quoted-string regex and **did not strip comments**, so an ordinary possessive in a `//` comment became a **phantom 58th permission** — found by stepping on it. Fixed + probes P-C1/P-C2/P-C3.
- **Files:** `supabase/migrations/20260828_owner_role_carries_authority.sql` (NEW, GATED) · `permissionManifest.ts` (3 status flips · `team.routeEnforced` true→false · `OWNER_DEFAULT_BUNDLE` 54→57) · `permissionManifest.test.ts` · `rosterAuthority.ts` + `rosterAuthority.test.ts` (**NEW, 47 probes**) · `auth/index.ts` · `invitations.ts` · `MemberConsole.tsx` · `cultivar-os/src/pages/Settings.tsx` · `shared/src/pages/Settings.tsx` · `serviceWriteFailure.ts` + `.test.ts` · `scripts/verify-authority-checks.mjs` · `scripts/verify-universals.mjs` · `r-b2-wired-since-declarations.json` (NEW) · `docs/owner-tests/owner-role-authority-full-surface-test.md` (**NEW — 8 cards, ALL `owed`**).

**Status:** 🟡 **BUILDER-COMPLETE · ✅ MIGRATION APPLIED 2026-08-29 (David) — the OWNER floor VERIFIED at 57 across ALL FOUR OWNER-role members. Owner-proof STILL OWED on the eight UI cards (0 of 8).** ⚠️ **The two are different claims and this line keeps them apart:** the floor being right proves the funnel materialised the STORED array and that it now agrees with the client's COMPUTED `OWNER_LOCKED_SET` — which is exactly the half a manifest flip alone would have missed. It does **not** prove an OWNER-role member reaches services, invitations and the roster through the real UI under RLS; that is what the eight cards are for. `npm run verify` **exit 0** · **42/42 files · 1702 assertions** (+1 file, +39) · **eslint 247 → 245** and **zero-row-writes 75 → 73**, both re-baselined to lock the win · knip/tsc unchanged · api/ **12/12 untouched, ZERO Vercel functions added** (an RPC is a postgres function, not a serverless one — §6 r11 is not engaged) · all `[TRACE:*]` ON. **RED-FIRST PROVEN AND COUNTED:** with the three consumer files reverted, `rosterAuthority.test.ts` reports **27 passed / 20 failed**; four mutants run (1 · 1 · 16 · 12). ⚠️ **STAGE 2 (AUTHORITY) IS NOT BUILT** — an OWNER-role actor still cannot assign a role. ⚠️ **Stage 1 refuses an OWNER-role INVITE from anyone but the account holder — Lightning's call, flagged for overrule**, so that every act creating an owner goes through Stage 2's one audited door. **Type:** capability (RLS/security) | **Vertical:** shared + cultivar.
**Cross-refs:** CLOSE-OUT-LEDGER **#228** · **R-22** · tech-debt **#90** (its blocker resolved) · story *Hand over the keys* · ruling 2026-07-30 (the owner branch removal this continues).

### Permission Funnel — no-op short-circuit + the honest `no_change` audit row (STD-023) — added 2026-07-28

**What:** `save_role_permissions` no longer writes when nothing changes — and still records that someone pressed Save. Migration `20260728c_funnel_no_op_short_circuit.sql` (**GATED**), replacing the 8-arg signature owned by `20260727_rbac_resource_action_flip.sql` (§6 r1 — a new file, never an edit of an applied one).
- **THE BEHAVIOUR (David's ruling 2026-07-28, tech-debt #74):** on `p_op='save'`, if the desired set equals the current tenant set, no `role_definitions` write and no member re-materialization occur; the call returns `applied=false, reason='no-op'`. **The `audit_log` row is still written, with `outcome='no_change'` and before/after recorded.** The reasoning, preserved in the migration header: *a Save that changed nothing is an OPERATOR ACT on a permissions surface — someone opened Roles, looked at a role, pressed Save. That is worth keeping. What is not acceptable is `outcome='success'` on `action='role.permissions_changed'` when nothing changed, because **the action name asserts an event that did not occur**.* `no_change` rows are ignorable by anything counting changes and present for anything reconstructing who touched what.
- **🔴 SET COMPARISON, NOT ARRAY COMPARISON.** jsonb array ordering is not guaranteed stable across a re-materialization, so `before <> after` on raw arrays reports a change on a **pure reordering** — a false positive that would reintroduce the defect from the other side. Implemented as two-way jsonb containment (order- and duplicate-insensitive by definition). **V3 is the check that distinguishes a set implementation from an array one:** same elements, reversed order, must still return `no-op`.
- **FOUR CASES THAT ARE DELIBERATELY NOT NO-OPS:** ① **no tenant row yet — saving a set identical to the floor MINTS the tenant override, the ONE-WAY DOOR.** Identical contents, profoundly different state; short-circuiting it would make the most consequential write in the system the most silent one. ② a **label/description** edit with an identical permission set — a short-circuit that swallowed it would be a new defect shipped inside the fix for an old one. ③ a **drifted member array** (the service-key/postgres path remains open by necessity, so drift is reachable; a save that repairs it changed something) — **⚠️ this case is an INTERPRETATION and is flagged: the ruling addressed desired-vs-resolved and did not cover a drifted member; resolved in the direction that keeps `no_change` truthful.** ④ `p_op <> 'save'`.
- **THE STATED COST:** `action='role.permissions_changed'` alone now includes no-ops, so **anything counting permission changes must filter on `outcome`.** Recorded in the migration header, on the owner-test card, and in `COMMENT ON COLUMN public.audit_log.outcome` — which named two values and now names three (a comment that undercounts its domain is the same artifact class this week was about).
- **STD-023 — the general form (STANDARDS v2.6):** *a guard the write does not depend on is advice, not a gate.* Twin of STD-022 one layer out — STD-022 asks *can this check ever fail?*, STD-023 asks *can the operation ever proceed without this check?* Corollary from the fix: **planner-dependent safety is not safety** — expressing a premise as the DRIVING RELATION rather than a trailing `WHERE … EXISTS` makes "no rows ⇒ not called" a property of the query shape, not a planner decision about pushing a qual below a lateral join against a VOLATILE function.
- **Files:** `supabase/migrations/20260728c_funnel_no_op_short_circuit.sql` (NEW, GATED) · `STANDARDS.md` STD-023 · `docs/owner-tests/team-permissions-full-surface-test.md` (**NEW card 8** + its negative twin; board 7 → 8).

**Status:** ✅ **APPLIED AND VERIFIED 2026-07-28 (David, at postgres) — BUILDER-COMPLETE · DEPLOYED · screen half NOT owner-proven.** V1 no-op → `applied=false, reason='no-op', outcome='no_change'`, 40 → 40 · **🔴 V3 REORDER — the same 40 elements in reversed order → still `no-op`. SET COMPARISON CONFIRMED, and this is the only probe an array-comparison build fails** (it returns `applied=true` there and passes everything else) · V4 label-change → `applied=true`, `member_id` populated, rolled back · `prosrc` contains the short-circuit. `npm run verify` exit 0 zero net-new; zero app code, zero api-fn (12/12). **🔴 THE THREE AUDIT ROWS ARE THE FIX'S OWN BEFORE/AFTER AND MUST NOT BE PRUNED:** `18:13:29 · success · 40 → 40` (the defect) → `18:45:08 v1-noop-probe · no_change` → `18:45:25 v3-reorder-probe · no_change`. Same tenant, same role, same counts — **the only difference is `outcome`, which is the whole property this migration establishes.** Removing them as "probe noise" would delete the demonstration that the guard is alive (STD-022, applied to the evidence rather than the check). **What remains is owner-test card 8 — the SCREEN path only**; the button may send a payload the probe did not (a re-ordered array, a label it thinks unchanged, a stale draft), which is precisely the BUILDER-COMPLETE → OWNER-PROVEN gap. **Type:** capability (RLS/security) | **Vertical:** shared + cultivar.
**Cross-refs:** CLOSE-OUT-LEDGER **#165** · tech-debt **#74** · STD-023 · STD-022 · ledger #163 (the gate failure that produced both).

### RBAC Transition — the four funnel calls EXECUTED, and the `assets:*` phantom cleanup — added 2026-07-28

**What:** The live execution of the `resource:verb` transition at tenant `f7ec5d67`, through the funnel, with an audit row per call — plus the fifth call the first four made necessary. Catalog-verified post-state (David, at postgres): **MANAGER member array 43 · tenant `role_definition` 43 · STAFF 10 · 10 · OWNER 6 (unchanged, still legacy) · floor OWNER 55 / MANAGER 28 / STAFF 10 untouched.**
- **THE FOUR CALLS AND THEIR `p_reason`s** (`docs/runbooks/2026-07-27-rbac-funnel-calls.sql`): `rbac-migration:rename` MANAGER 13 legacy → **35** (R-A decomposition, no authority added) · `rbac-migration:grant` MANAGER 35 → **43** (the eight David chose; **`orders:delete` + `order_discount:apply` are recorded DEPARTURES from spec §5's read/create/update bundle — owner calls, not defaults**) · `rbac-migration:rename` STAFF 3 → 5 (**MINTS the tenant STAFF override — a one-way door; this tenant's STAFF stops tracking the floor from that write**) · `rbac-migration:staff-narrow` STAFF 5 → 10 (**additive in the array; the REVOCATION is delivered by the migration's §1.15 policy split, not by this call** — an auditor reading the row alone would conclude nothing was taken away).
- **THE ARITHMETIC RECONCILES, AND THE PLAN WAS THE ONE THAT WAS WRONG.** Transition plan §10.2 predicted decomposition **36** and `36 ∪ 8 = 44`; the runbook executed **35** and **43**; live is **43**. The off-by-one is the decomposition count — **not** a string that failed to land; the union is exact, zero overlap. The **10 → 5 reading trap is RESOLVED, not open**: tenant STAFF 10, floor STAFF 10, member 10 — the audit row's `n_before` read the resolved template rather than the member's 3.
- **🔴 THE FINDING — THE RENAME MINTED THREE PHANTOM STRINGS, AND EVERY CHECK WE OWN IS GREEN ON THEM.** The MANAGER holds `assets:create/read/update` for a resource with **no table** (`business_assets` → `cost_objects`, 2026-06-15), **no policy, no manifest entry**. Proven arithmetically that **`20260727b_align_floor_assets_retired.sql` is UNAPPLIED**: the live floor is `20260727_align_floor_to_bundles.sql` **exactly on all three roles** (that file sets 55/28/10 *with* `assets:*` on OWNER+MANAGER; 727b sets 52/25/10 without — STAFF matching at 10 in both is the control row, STAFF never held them), and no migration after it touches `role_definitions`. The alias prune lives in that same unapplied file, so the decomposition (`20260727_rbac_resource_action_flip.sql:518`, `JOIN … ON a.implies_perm = legacy.s` — the **surviving new-checked direction**) still emits all three, and R-B2's output filter names eight declared-unwired strings, none of them `assets:*`. **The next rename at any tenant reproduces it deterministically.** The surviving alias set is **THREE, not six** — #155's rename-only correction had already deleted every legacy-checked row on a 1→many split.
- **THE FIX IS TWO HALVES AND NEITHER WORKS ALONE.** The **migration** owns the floor + alias rows (`business_id IS NULL`); the **funnel** owns the tenant row + member arrays, because those are only ever written through `save_role_permissions` with an audit row (#152 — the §1 trigger refuses a direct JWT UPDATE, so a hand-cleanup would be the side door we closed on ourselves). **CALL 5** carries `rbac-cleanup:assets-retired` and a **run-time halt gate** that re-reads every premise and RAISEs rather than proceeding: refuses if the alias rows survive (the next rename would undo the cleanup), if the floor is not 25, if the tenant holds no `assets:*` (**never write an audit row for a no-op**), or if any member array has diverged from the template (WIPE-not-merge would overwrite it silently). The row reads **43 → 40 and no authority is lost** — the strings gate nothing; `/assets` + `/pmi` moved to `costs:read`.
- **OWNER'S 6 LEGACY STRINGS ARE PHASE 6 — confirmed a plan, not an omission** (`rbac-backfill-contract.md:3`). Pinned in both directions: at runtime they gate nothing (`has_permission` short-circuits on `owner_id`, `20260726_permission_alias_layer.sql:313-315`) **but they block Contract**, whose zero-check is *"zero members hold a legacy string"* (card N-14). Behaviour-neutral and gate-blocking at once.
- **Files:** `supabase/migrations/20260727b_align_floor_assets_retired.sql` (AMENDED — header records the missed window with its proof; prune scope corrected 6→3 with the reason; V2 rescoped to `business_id IS NULL` because unscoped it returns the tenant's strings and reads as a failure of a migration behaving correctly; V2b positive control added) · `docs/runbooks/2026-07-28-rbac-assets-retirement-cleanup.sql` (**NEW** — CALL 5 + gates) · `docs/runbooks/2026-07-27-rbac-funnel-calls.sql` (the four) · `docs/decisions/2026-07-27-rbac-transition-execution-plan.md` §11.5b (**NEW** — the Contract blind spot).

- **✅ APPLIED AND VERIFIED 2026-07-28 — and the sixth audit row is a second finding.** Post-state at the catalog: **floor MANAGER 25 · tenant template 40 · member array 40 · zero `assets:*` on any surface · zero `assets` rows in `permission_aliases`.** Six audit rows, every transition named: `13→35` rename · `35→43` grant · `10→5` rename (STAFF) · `5→10` staff-narrow · **`43→40` assets-retired** · **`40→40` no-op**. **🔴 THE HALT GATE DID NOT HOLD.** The file was re-run 88 seconds after the real work, the tenant held zero `assets:*`, the gate's own rule said refuse, and the call executed anyway with outcome `success` — **an audit row recording a change where none occurred asserts an event nobody caused**, in the one artifact whose entire value is being evidence. **From source, two independent structural bypasses, neither operator error:** ① **the guard was a statement BESIDE the write, not a condition OF it** — separate top-level statements with no transaction binding them, so whether the RAISE stopped the call was a property of the CLIENT (psql without `ON_ERROR_STOP` runs past a failed statement; highlighting the call alone never reaches the gate). *A guard the write does not depend on is advice, not a gate.* ② **`save_role_permissions` itself accepts a no-op** — no `IS DISTINCT FROM` check exists anywhere in the function; it re-materializes and appends the `audit_log` row unconditionally, **so pressing Save on `/team → Roles` with nothing changed writes a `role.permissions_changed` row today.** **FIXED HERE (runbook half):** explicit `BEGIN … COMMIT` so a RAISE in either gate rolls the call back regardless of client behaviour, **and the premise is now the DRIVING SUBQUERY of the write** — zero rows in ⇒ the LATERAL function is never invoked, a property of the query shape rather than a planner choice about a volatile lateral join. A re-run now halts at STEP 1 and writes nothing. **FLAGGED NOT TAKEN (funnel half): tech-debt #74** — short-circuit when resolved == current and every active member agrees, returning the existing `applied=false, reason='no-op'` shape with no audit row; David rules, it is a migration touching all 13 write sites' shared entry point. **The `40 → 40` row is deliberately NOT deleted — it is the evidence the gate needed fixing.**

**Status:** ⏳ **BUILDER-COMPLETE · DEPLOYED · MIGRATION + CALL 5 APPLIED AND CATALOG-VERIFIED · NOT OWNER-PROVEN — owner-test board reads 0 of 42.** Zero app code, zero api-fn (12/12). The database work is closed and proven at the catalog; what remains is the **42 cards** on `docs/owner-tests/rbac-resource-action-full-surface-test.md` — the on-screen behaviour under a real MANAGER session. Only David's live run moves a card (OP-14). **Type:** capability (RLS/security) | **Vertical:** shared + cultivar.

### Permission Alias Layer (RBAC Phase 0) + the legacy-is-rename-only CORRECTION — added 2026-07-27

**What:** The migration-window bridge between the legacy permission vocabulary and `resource:verb`, so Phases 1–6 of the RBAC refit can land in any order without a gate ever returning the wrong answer. `public.permission_aliases(from_perm, implies_perm)` + an alias-aware `has_permission` / `has_permission_for` (both still `SECURITY DEFINER` + `STABLE` + `search_path=''`; the direct containment test is evaluated FIRST so the alias lookup is skipped on the hit path — a per-row RLS cost requirement, not a note). Seeded from `permissionManifest.ts` `ALIAS_PAIRS`. Migration `20260726_permission_alias_layer.sql` (**GATED**), shipped `74dcff4` with the manifest, the 42-card rebuild, and capP in WARN.
- **THE SEMANTICS (read this before touching a row):** the table is **DIRECTED and ONE HOP** — `from_perm` is the string being **CHECKED** (`p_perm`), `implies_perm` is a string the member may **HOLD** that satisfies it. No transitive closure, so no chain of aliases can compound into a grant nobody wrote.
- **THE CORRECTION (`2bea456`, migration `20260726_permission_alias_legacy_rename_only.sql`, GATED) — a defect FOUND LIVE 2026-07-26, closed by hand the same day, then migrated.** A unique index `permission_aliases_one_reverse_target` had been placed on the NEW-checked side and was wrong twice over: it **forbade a legitimate row** (`pricing_recipe:update` is the replacement of BOTH `manage_settings` AND `view_pricing_config`, so it correctly carries two satisfiers) while **leaving the real escalation open** — a gate checking a 1→many SPLIT (`view_costs` → 14 shards) was satisfied by holding **any one** shard, so an `inventory:read` holder passed every surviving `view_costs` policy (`cost_objects`, `receipts`). Four idempotent statements: drop the wrong index · re-assert the lost row · DELETE the 39 legacy-checked split rows (9 legacy strings) · CREATE the **partial** unique index `permission_aliases_legacy_is_rename_only ON (from_perm) WHERE from_perm NOT LIKE '%:%'`.
- **THE RULE IT ENCODES — ASYMMETRIC BY DESIGN:** MANY satisfiers are **correct** new-checked (a coarse legacy string that decomposes into a fine one genuinely implies it); **AT MOST ONE, and only a pure RENAME**, legacy-checked (holding one of fourteen shards is not holding the bundle). The 7 renames survive both directions; the 9 splits resolve one direction only. Live shape after: **46 rows / 45 distinct new-checked · 7 / 7 legacy-checked · 53 total.** `COMMENT ON TABLE` corrected where it read "seeded BOTH DIRECTIONS"; `COMMENT ON INDEX` carries the rule where the next person is standing.
- **CONSEQUENCE — invariant (ii) is now LOAD-BEARING.** A premature Phase 6 backfill no longer *widens* authority, it **REVOKES** it. Enforced as new **R-D** in `docs/decisions/2026-07-26-rbac-backfill-contract.md`: a per-capability precondition proved by whole-schema `pg_policies` **AND** a source grep (route/tile/api) — zero rows and zero hits, or that capability's backfill does not run. **`manage_orders` is the case proving the RLS half alone is insufficient** — 4 api gates, 0 policies (`submit.ts` 238/1005/1223/1292 via `callerCanManageOrders`), so a catalog-only check would call it safe while the gate is fully alive.
- **Files:** `supabase/migrations/20260726_permission_alias_layer.sql` (NEW, `74dcff4`) · `supabase/migrations/20260726_permission_alias_legacy_rename_only.sql` (NEW, `2bea456`) · `packages/shared/src/auth/permissionManifest.ts` · `docs/decisions/2026-07-26-rbac-backfill-contract.md` (R-D) · `scripts/verify-universals.mjs` (capP, WARN mode — unchanged by the correction, findings measured unmoved at 20).

**Status:** ⏳ **BUILDER-COMPLETE, both migrations GATED, OWNER-PROVE OWED.** `npm run verify` exit 0 zero net-new. The layer's V1/V2 are SUPERSEDED by the correction's W1/W2+W6; run **W1–W7** after applying (sharpest: **W2** no legacy string carries a second satisfier · **W3** the wrong index gone / the right one present · **W5** neutrality *measured* — zero active members hold any `:` string yet · **W7** a rename round-trips TRUE while a split shard grants the bundle FALSE). ⚠️ **`ALIAS_PAIRS` in `permissionManifest.ts` is KNOWINGLY STALE** — it still emits both directions unconditionally and its inline "forward"/"reverse" comments are INVERTED relative to the resolver; regenerating from it would reintroduce the 39 rows (the partial index now refuses them). Scope-split to a follow-on commit by David's ruling; **until then the DB is right and the TS register is wrong.** **Type:** capability (RLS/security) | **Vertical:** shared + cultivar.
**Cross-refs:** CLOSE-OUT-LEDGER #155 (correction) + #154 (plan) · `docs/resource-action-permission-spec.md` v3 §8 · `docs/decisions/2026-07-26-rbac-build-plan.md` · `docs/decisions/2026-07-26-rbac-backfill-contract.md` (R-A/R-B/R-C/**R-D**) · `docs/standards/permission-enforcement-map.md` · STD-020.

### Manager Visibility Pass + Permission Enforcement Map (STD-020) — added 2026-07-24

**What:** The READ/gate half of the permission system the D-50 funnel (#152) did not reach. Closes six gaps David found live as a MANAGER (2026-07-24, tenant f7ec5d67, member df7723be), all one root cause: a capability is gated in up to three independent layers (ROUTE `PermissionRoute` → TABLE RLS → FUNCTION RPC) with nothing requiring them to agree. **STD-020 (added + activated, STANDARDS.md v2.5):** one permission = one capability, checked at EVERY layer it touches.
- **Six additive gaps** (migration `20260724_manager_visibility_gaps.sql`, **GATED**, ZERO modified policies): (1) `service_offerings_member` SELECT on `is_active_member` — the sell-side catalog (add-ons/transport/netting) is not a financial secret; writes stay owner-only. (2) `orders_member_select` + (3) `order_items_member` SELECT on `has_permission('view_orders')` — a manager reads the orders they took, and the committed derivation reads 132 units as COMMITTED not AVAILABLE; extended to the whole order-read family (`order_service_selections`/`order_compliance_records`) to avoid the 20260709-documented asymmetry. **This retires the third fake pill — `view_orders` now means something.** (4) **NARROW `get_business_tax_rate(uuid)`** — SECURITY DEFINER `search_path=''`, membership-checked, returns ONLY `config->>'taxRate'` (never the pricing recipe); wired via shared `fetchTaxRate` → `CartReview`, fixing the untaxed-$544 invoice and the D-9 "not identified" copy. (5) `/customers` route re-gated from the unholdable literal `owner-only` → `PERMISSIONS.VIEW_CUSTOMERS` (the string `customers_member` RLS already checks). (6) two dead pills (`manage_customers`/`view_reports`) hidden via new `UNWIRED_REGISTRY_PERMISSIONS` (shared, ONE source); MANAGER role-card pill-count counts only RENDERABLE pills (12→11, `override_maintenance` retained in the array but not counted).
- **BUILD 2 — the reconciliation artifact:** `docs/standards/permission-enforcement-map.md` — one row per capability, columns route/table/function + explicit AGREE, with its own binding close-out gate. Flags the next-six disagreements (social_drafts/campaigns open-at-door-locked-at-vault; deliveries/pmi string mismatch). The R5b map was deferred at #152 and re-ranked on live evidence — the deferral was the error.

(D-012, MB_D-012). **OWNER-PROVEN owed:** David sees the registry-driven grid live — owner sees all live tiles incl. the 6 financial surfaces; a Staff (no view_costs) session sees neither the cost tiles nor today's-sales/inventory-value readouts.

A COUNT, NOT A CAP, AND NOT A FIX.** ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY NOT CHAINED INTO `npm run verify`, and the reason is the point: minting a cap before David rules would answer a ruling with a constant** — which is the exact thing the measurement exists to prevent. It is a one-off, run by hand, and it changes nothing. **THE CLASS IT MEASURES: a registry row can be INTERNALLY VALID and still be FALSE ABOUT THE WORLD.** `verify-tile-fields` (the entry above) passes over every instance **correctly** — it asserts a row is COMPLETE and VALID against its own schema and reconciles catalog↔tile in both directions, and it has **no assertion comparing one row to ANOTHER ROW DESCRIBING THE SAME THING**, nor a declared `section` against an actual `route`. David found two instances by looking at screenshots on a phone: `discounts`(live, `/discounts`) vs `contractor_tiers`(planned, holds the `module_key`) on the SAME `pricing_recipe:update`, and `nav_accounting` (breadcrumb *Admin /*, URL `/settings/`). 🔴 **THE NUMBERS — Q1 = 12 contradicting pairs · Q2 = 22 section/route disagreements of 27 routed nav nodes · Q3 = 5 modules paired to a tile no renderer draws · `businesses` = 7 undeclared write paths.** The two found by eye are **1 of 12** and **1 of 22**. **METHOD — esbuild-EVALUATE, not text-parse** (precedent: `verify:write-wall`): the registry is bundled with `lucide-react` aliased to an inert stub and imported, so the REAL `dashboardTiles()` and `navRoute()` are **called, not re-implemented** — RENDERABLE and tileKey-route resolution cannot drift from the code they describe, and no status/placement/section list is typed anywhere in the script. **The proven `verify-tile-fields` walker was the FIRST choice and was NOT reused, for a stated reason:** it is not exported (only `scan` is) and the module has no `import.meta.main` guard, so importing it executes its probes, its scan and its `process.exit` — reuse meant editing a live cap mid-measurement, and copying meant forking the one parser whose failure mode is documented in its own header (§6 r8 weighed, not ignored). **12 PROBES, ALL GREEN:** P1 row-count (evaluated 33 == an INDEPENDENT text count 33 — so the 2026-08-01 zero-row false-green cannot recur silently), P7 negative control (a clean fixture reports 0/0/0, without which the planted-bads are satisfied by a script that flags everything), P11 (Q3's sibling clause is enforced, not decorative), **P12 module-cache** (node caches ESM by URL — a reused outfile would have made every planted-bad pass while measuring the wrong file). **PLANTED-BAD AGAINST THE REAL FILE, not only fixtures:** three synthetic rows → 33→34 in BOTH count methods, Q1 12→13, Q2 22→23, Q3 5→6, **each NAMED by key**; then restored, `git diff` EMPTY and `diff -q` IDENTICAL, re-run back to 33/12/22/5. **STD-021 honoured — corpus and method named, and the required *what this CANNOT see* section lists NINE blind spots**, including that Q1 pairs on `required_permission` ALONE (had David's two rows also disagreed on their permission, this measurement would report ZERO for the defect he actually found) and that it never opened `router.tsx`, so it cannot say whether a route in Q2 even exists. **NOTHING WAS FIXED AND NOTHING RULED — three counts and four decisions are owed to David**, including the prior question Q2 depends on: *is a `section` that is not a URL prefix a defect at all?* (`dashboard` has ZERO children under `/dashboard`; `admin` has two under `/admin` and seven not.)

## Ignition OS — Workflow Modules (2026-06-09 Reality Audit)

**Audit date:** 2026-06-09 | **Standard:** STD-010 (opaque name = debt; discovered build = catalogue it) | **Scope:** all .jsx/.js in `packages/ignition-os/` (root + modules/ + hooks/) | **Full report:** `docs/audits/ignition-reality-audit-2026-06-09.md`

> **Root-cause notes before the table:**
> - **DARK root cause:** `VITE_API_URL` not set in ignition-os Vercel project → every `AIEngine.call()` returns `{ ok: false }`. Railway running but receives zero Vercel traffic.
> - **BROKEN root cause:** `20260602_ignition_drop_team_tables.sql` dropped `pin_resets`, `shop_invites`, `member_devices`. Only `shop_members` was recreated (`20260603_recreate_shop_members.sql`). The other three tables have no recreate migration.
> - **SPLIT-BRAIN root cause:** `IgnitionIntake` writes customers to Supabase `customers`. `IgnitionCRM` reads from DataBridge `customers_directory`. These are entirely separate stores — intake customers never appear in CRM.

**Authoritative detail:** [`docs/audits/ignition-reality-audit-2026-06-09.md`](audits/ignition-reality-audit-2026-06-09.md) — full module table, workflow chain, RBAC, and DataBridge orphaned keys.

### Workflow Chain Summary

The RO chain is **mostly operational** end-to-end. Key degradation points:

```
Step 1 (Intake) → ✅ Step 2 (Queue) → ✅ Step 3 (Eval) → ⚠️ DTC decode (3 codes only, rest DARK)
→ Step 4 (Estimate: manual works, AI skeleton DARK, auto-PO DARK)
→ Step 5 (Pricing: PORT path DataBridge, Estimate path Supabase — NOT SYNCHRONIZED)
→ Step 6 (Customer Auth: ✅ digital signature)
→ Step 7 (Kiosk: ✅ labor/repair/custody)
→ Step 8 (Invoice: ✅ Supabase; PDF = stub; QB = DARK + no Vercel functions)
```

**QB note:** Cultivar OS has `api/qbo/*` Vercel functions. Ignition OS has NONE. QB is not just DARK — the server-side functions do not exist for Ignition.

**Two parallel estimate paths (incompatible):** `IgnitionEstimate.jsx` (Supabase `estimates/estimate_line_items`) and `IgnitionPort.jsx` (DataBridge `active_job_context`). The same workflow step has two separate, unsynchronized data stores. Customer authorization via `CustomerApprovalPortal` works from both but writes to `customer_authorizations` (Supabase) in both cases.

### RBAC — 19 Permissions, 4 Role Presets, Client-Side Only

Defined in `IgnitionAdmin.jsx ALL_PERMISSIONS`. Enforced by `CoreApp AccessGatekeeper` which checks DataBridge `current_user.permissions`. No Supabase RLS enforcement. Notable gap: `IgnitionCompliance.jsx` has `requiredPermissions={[]}` in CoreApp routing — any logged-in user (including CUSTOMER role) can access the DOT inspection form.

### DataBridge Orphaned Keys

| Type | Key | Problem |
|---|---|---|
| Orphaned write | `margin_change_log` | Written by `DataBridge.setMarginConfig()`. No module reads or displays it. |
| Orphaned write | `pending_users` | Written by IgnitionOmni legacy staff enrollment. Only `EnrollmentCatch` reads it — via an orphaned flow. |
| Orphaned read | `inventory_items` | IgnitionOmni reads for stats. IgnitionStok reads from Supabase `inventory` — not DataBridge. Inventory value tile = always $0. |
| Orphaned read | `fleet_units` | IgnitionHub reads. No module in web build writes real fleet unit data. Hub shows empty GPS grid. |
| Orphaned read | `labor_guide` | `DataBridge.getLaborGuide()` always returns hardcoded defaults — no module has ever written a real labor guide. |
